\displaystyle+\frac{1}{400}\sum_{\alpha+|\beta|\leq B}\frac{2^{-\mathtt{C}\beta}\left\langle k\right\rangle^{\alpha}}{\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{2\beta}}\bigg(\nu\mathcal{A}[\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M}Z^{\beta}\hat{f}]+\nu a_{\nu,k}\mathcal{A}[\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}\nabla_{v}Z^{\beta}\hat{f}]

(4.26)

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt+b_{\nu,k}|k|^{2}\left\lVert\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}Z^{\beta}\hat{f}\right\rVert^{2}\bigg)

(4.27)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\alpha+|\beta|\leq B}\frac{2^{-\mathtt{C}\beta}\left\langle k\right\rangle^{\alpha}}{\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{2\beta}}a_{\nu,k}\left|\left\langle\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}Z^{\beta}(ik\hat{f}),\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}\nabla_{v}Z^{\beta}\hat{f}\right\rangle\right|.

\diamond

Bound on the mixed inner product.
The last error term appearing in (
4.27
) is the most delicate to control. Indeed, we do not have
\nu
as a smallness parameter and it is the first term where we have to explicitly deal with the softness of the potential. To overcome the latter issue, it is crucial to use the fact that
v
-derivatives are controlled with weaker velocity weights. For instance, in the Landau case, one has
s=1
and
q_{-3,1}=-3/2
. Thus, we can directly control this term combining the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality with
a^{2}_{\nu,k}/b_{\nu,k}\ll\nu
; the proof for
s=1
is omitted for brevity, as it is more straightforward (see also
[
21
]
, where
q_{-3,1}=-4
though).
We begin by noting the following lower bounds, obtained from Proposition
3.1

\displaystyle\nu\mathcal{A}[\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M}Z^{\beta}\hat{f}]+\nu a_{\nu,k}\mathcal{A}[\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}\nabla_{v}Z^{\beta}\hat{f}]

(4.28)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\gtrsim\nu\left\lVert\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M}Z^{\beta}\hat{f}\right\rVert^{2}_{H^{s}_{\gamma/2}}+\nu a_{\nu,k}\left\lVert\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}\nabla_{v}Z^{\beta}\hat{f}\right\rVert^{2}_{H^{s}_{\gamma/2}},

For a general
0<s<1
, we have to handle carefully the anisotropy of the dissipation. We consider a dyadic decomposition of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
and apply the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality to get

(4.29)

\displaystyle a_{\nu,k}

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}Z^{\beta}(ik\hat{f}),\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}\nabla_{v}Z^{\beta}\hat{f}\right\rangle\right|\leq

(4.30)

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}a_{\nu,k}|k|\left\lVert\mathbbm{1}_{\{2^{j}\leq\left\langle v\right\rangle\leq 2^{j+1}\}}\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}Z^{\beta}\hat{f}\right\rVert\left\lVert\mathbbm{1}_{\{2^{j}\leq\left\langle v\right\rangle\leq 2^{j+1}\}}\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}\nabla_{v}Z^{\beta}\hat{f}\right\rVert

(4.31)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\,\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\mathcal{R}_{j}.

Using the Young’s inequality, we get

(4.32)

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{j}\leq\,

\displaystyle\frac{b_{\nu,k}}{1600}|k|^{2}\left\lVert\mathbbm{1}_{\{2^{j}\leq\left\langle v\right\rangle\leq 2^{j+1}\}}\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}Z^{\beta}\hat{f}\right\rVert^{2}

(4.33)

\displaystyle+1600\frac{a^{2}_{\nu,k}}{b_{\nu,k}}\left\lVert\mathbbm{1}_{\{2^{j}\leq\left\langle v\right\rangle\leq 2^{j+1}\}}\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}\nabla_{v}Z^{\beta}\hat{f}\right\rVert^{2}.

By the choice of the coefficients in (
2.4
), notice that

(4.34)

\frac{a^{2}_{\nu,k}}{b_{\nu,k}}=\frac{a_{0}^{1+s}}{b_{0}}\nu a^{1-s}_{\nu,k}.

Combining (
4.34
) with the Gagliardo-Nirenberg inequality, since
q_{\gamma,s}\leq 0
, we obtain

(4.35)

\displaystyle 1600

\displaystyle\frac{a^{2}_{\nu,k}}{b_{\nu,k}}\left\lVert\mathbbm{1}_{\{2^{j}\leq\left\langle v\right\rangle\leq 2^{j+1}\}}\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}\nabla_{v}Z^{\beta}\hat{f}\right\rVert^{2}\leq

(4.36)

\displaystyle C\frac{a_{0}^{1+s}}{b_{0}}\bigg(2^{-(j+1)\gamma}\nu a_{\nu,k}\left\lVert\mathbbm{1}_{\{2^{j}\leq\left\langle v\right\rangle\leq 2^{j+1}\}}\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}\nabla_{v}Z^{\beta}\hat{f}\right\rVert^{2}_{H^{s}_{\gamma/2}}\bigg)^{1-s}

(4.37)

\displaystyle\times\bigg(2^{-(j+1)\gamma+2jq_{\gamma,s}}\nu\left\lVert\mathbbm{1}_{\{2^{j}\leq\left\langle v\right\rangle\leq 2^{j+1}\}}\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M}Z^{\beta}\hat{f}\right\rVert^{2}_{H^{s}_{\gamma/2}}\bigg)^{s}.

From the inequality above, we deduce that

(4.38)

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{+\infty}\mathcal{R}_{j}\leq\,\frac{b_{\nu,k}}{1600}|k|^{2}\left\lVert\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}Z^{\beta}\hat{f}\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle+C\frac{a_{0}^{1+s}}{b_{0}}\bigg(\nu a_{\nu,k}\left\lVert\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}\nabla_{v}Z^{\beta}\hat{f}\right\rVert^{2}_{H^{s}_{\gamma/2}}\bigg)^{1-s}\bigg(\nu\left\lVert\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M}Z^{\beta}\hat{f}\right\rVert^{2}_{H^{s}_{\gamma/2}}\bigg)^{s}\sum_{j=0}^{+\infty}2^{-(j+1)\gamma}2^{2sjq_{\gamma,s}}.

The series above is convergent for any
q_{\gamma,s}<\gamma/(2s)
, which is guaranteed by the choice we made in (
2.3
). Thus, using (
4.28
),
we conclude

(4.39)

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{+\infty}\mathcal{R}_{j}\leq\,

\displaystyle\frac{b_{\nu,k}}{1600}|k|^{2}\left\lVert\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}Z^{\beta}\hat{f}\right\rVert^{2}

(4.40)

\displaystyle+\widetilde{C}\frac{a_{0}^{1+s}}{b_{0}}\left(\nu a_{\nu,k}\mathcal{A}[\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}\nabla_{v}Z^{\beta}\hat{f}]+\nu\mathcal{A}[\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M}Z^{\beta}\hat{f}]\right).

The term above can be absorbed on the left-hand side of (
4.27
) upon choosing

(4.41)

a_{0}^{1+s}\ll b_{0}.

By the definition of the coefficients in (
2.4
), satisfying the constraints (
4.17
), (
4.19
), (
4.23
), (
4.25
) and (
4.41
) is equivalent to imposing

(4.42)

\begin{split}&\mathtt{C}\gg 1,\qquad\left(\frac{\nu}{|k|}\right)^{\frac{2s}{1+2s}}\leq\delta_{0}^{\frac{2s}{1+2s}}\ll b_{0},\qquad a_{0}\delta_{0}^{\frac{2(1+s)}{(1+2s)}}\ll b_{0},\\
&b_{0}^{2}\ll a_{0},\qquad a_{0}^{1+s}\ll b_{0}.\end{split}

Hence,
\mathtt{C}
is simply some sufficiently large (universal) constant to absorb the error terms coming from the commutators of
\mathcal{L}
. To satisfy the restrictions on
a_{0},b_{0},\delta_{0}
, let
0<\kappa_{0}\ll 1
. One can then choose

(4.43)

a_{0}=\frac{1}{1000}\kappa_{0}^{\frac{1}{1+2s}},\qquad b_{0}=\sqrt{\kappa_{0}a_{0}},\qquad\delta_{0}=\frac{1}{1000}\min\big\{\kappa_{0}^{\frac{1+s}{2s}},\kappa_{0}^{\frac{s}{2(1+s)}}\big\}.

It is not hard to verify that this choice satisfies all the constraints in (
4.42
). Finally, using (
4.40
) in (
4.27
), we arrive at

(4.44)

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}E^{e.d.}_{M,B}+\frac{1}{1600}\sum_{\alpha+|\beta|\leq B}\frac{2^{-\mathtt{C}\beta}\left\langle k\right\rangle^{\alpha}}{\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{2\beta}}\bigg(

\displaystyle\nu\mathcal{A}[\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M}Z^{\beta}\hat{f}]+\nu a_{\nu,k}\mathcal{A}[\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}\nabla_{v}Z^{\beta}\hat{f}]

(4.45)

\displaystyle+\frac{b_{\nu,k}}{1600}|k|^{2}\left\lVert\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}Z^{\beta}\hat{f}\right\rVert^{2}\bigg)\leq 0.

In view of the definition of
\mathcal{D}^{e.d.}_{M,B}
(
2.5
), the monotonicity estimate (
4.1
) is proved.
∎

4.2.
Monotonicity estimate in the Taylor dispersion regime

We now turn our attention to the the proof of (
4.2
). As explained in Section
2.1
, we have to combine the micro-macro energy approach and the hypocoercivity scheme. We first deal with the macroscopic quantities in Section
4.2.1
and then we present the bounds for microscopic ones in Section
4.2.2
.

4.2.1.
Bounds on the macroscopic quantities

Recall that

(4.46)

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{P}f=\left(\rho(t,x)+\mathsf{m}(t,x)\cdot v+\mathsf{e}(t,x)(|v|^{2}-d)\right)\sqrt{\mu},

where
(\rho,\mathsf{m},\mathsf{e})
satisfy the hydrodynamic system (
2.7a
)-(
2.7c
). To gain dissipation for the macroscopic variables, it is necessary to introduce the equations satisfied by
\Theta[(I-\boldsymbol{P})f],\Lambda[(I-\boldsymbol{P})f]
(that are terms appearing on the right-hand side of (
2.7b
) and (
2.7c
) respectively) which are given

(4.47)

\displaystyle{\partial}_{t}(\Theta[(I-\boldsymbol{P})f]+2\mathsf{e}I)+\nabla_{x}\mathsf{m}+(\nabla_{x}\mathsf{m})^{T}=-\Theta[ik\cdot v(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}+\nu\mathcal{L}(I-\boldsymbol{P})f],

(4.48)

\displaystyle{\partial}_{t}\Lambda[(I-\boldsymbol{P})f]+\nabla_{x}\mathsf{e}=-\Lambda[ik\cdot v(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}+\nu\mathcal{L}(I-\boldsymbol{P})f].

We recall that
\Theta,\Lambda
are the higher-order moments projections defined in (
2.8
) and (
2.9
) respectively.
Looking at the structure of the hydrodynamic system (
2.7a
)-(
2.7c
) and (
4.47
)-(
4.48
), it is natural to try to exploit mixed inner products to recover dissipation for the macroscopic variables. Indeed, loosely speaking, one has the following

(4.49)

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}(\mathsf{m}\cdot\nabla_{x}\rho)+|\nabla_{x}\rho|^{2}=\text{error terms}

(4.50)

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}((\Theta[(I-\boldsymbol{P})f]+2\mathsf{e}I):(\nabla_{x}\mathsf{m}+(\nabla_{x}\mathsf{m})^{T}))+|\nabla_{x}\mathsf{m}+(\nabla_{x}\mathsf{m})^{T}|^{2}=\text{error terms}

(4.51)

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}(\Lambda[(I-\boldsymbol{P})f]\cdot\nabla_{x}\mathsf{e})+|\nabla_{x}\mathsf{e}|^{2}=\text{error terms}

The idea of using mixed inner products to recover dissipation dates back at least to the PhD thesis of Kawashima
[
49
]
and has been successfully exploited in many different problems
[
7
,
43
,
39
,
61
,
34
]
.
However, we also need to recover dissipation for terms involving
Z
-derivatives. Thus, we first need the following equivalence.

Lemma 4.3
.

For any
\beta\geq 0
, the following inequalities holds true

(4.52)

\displaystyle\lVert Z^{\beta}\widehat{\boldsymbol{P}f}\rVert_{L^{2}_{v}}^{2}\leq 100|(tk)^{2\beta}||(\hat{\rho},\hat{\mathsf{m}},\hat{\mathsf{e}})|^{2}+C_{1}\sum_{0\leq|\tilde{\beta}|\leq|\beta|-1}|(tk)^{2\tilde{\beta}}||(\hat{\rho},\hat{\mathsf{m}},\hat{\mathsf{e}})|^{2},

(4.53)

\displaystyle\lVert Z^{\beta}\widehat{\boldsymbol{P}f}\rVert_{L^{2}_{v}}^{2}\geq\frac{1}{2}|(tk)^{2\beta}||(\hat{\rho},\hat{\mathsf{m}},\hat{\mathsf{e}})|^{2}-C_{2}\sum_{0\leq|\tilde{\beta}|\leq|\beta|-1}|(tk)^{2\tilde{\beta}}||(\hat{\rho},\hat{\mathsf{m}},\hat{\mathsf{e}})|^{2}.

From this lemma we deduce that we can consider
\lVert Z^{\beta}\widehat{\boldsymbol{P}f}\rVert_{L^{2}_{v}}
as being equivalent to
|tk|^{\beta}|(\hat{\rho},\hat{\mathsf{m}},\hat{\mathsf{e}})|
up to lower order terms.

Proof.

When
\beta=0
the equivalence is a direct consequence of the orthogonality in
L^{2}_{v}
of

(\sqrt{\mu},v_{i}\sqrt{\mu},(|v|^{2}-d)\sqrt{\mu}),

which is a basis for the kernel of
\mathcal{L}
.
When
|\beta|>0
, notice that

\displaystyle Z^{\beta}(\boldsymbol{P}f)

\displaystyle=\sum_{|\beta_{1}|+|\beta_{2}|=|\beta|}C_{\beta_{1},\beta_{2}}\bigg(((t\nabla_{x})^{\beta_{1}}\rho)(\nabla_{v}^{\beta_{2}}\sqrt{\mu})+((t\nabla_{x})^{\beta_{1}}\mathsf{e})(\nabla_{v}^{\beta_{2}}((|v|^{2}-d)\sqrt{\mu}))

(4.54)

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{j=1}^{d}((t\nabla_{x})^{\beta_{1}}\mathsf{m}_{j})(\nabla_{v}^{\beta_{2}}(v_{j}\sqrt{\mu}))\bigg)

(4.55)

\displaystyle=((t\nabla_{x})^{\beta}\rho)\sqrt{\mu}+((t\nabla_{x})^{\beta}\mathsf{e})((|v|^{2}-s)\sqrt{\mu})+\sum_{j=1}^{d}((t\nabla_{x})^{\beta}\mathsf{m}_{j})(v_{j}\sqrt{\mu})+\mathcal{I}_{\widetilde{\beta}},

where the term
\mathcal{I}_{\widetilde{\beta}}
is what it remains from the sum when
|\beta_{2}|\geq 1
.
Using again the orthogonality condition, the proof of (
4.52
) follows by the identity above and Cauchy-Schwarz inequality.
∎

The dissipation for the macroscopic variables is recovered from the mixed inner product defined in (
2.12
). In particular, we have the following adaptation of the estimate originally obtained in
[
43
,
34
]
.

Lemma 4.4
.

Let
\mathcal{M}
be defined as in (
2.12
) and assume that
b_{2}\ll b_{1}\ll 1
. Then, there exists a universal constant
0<\tilde{c}<1
such that

(4.56)

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\mathcal{M}

\displaystyle+\tilde{c}\sum_{0\leq|\beta|\leq B}\frac{|k|^{2}}{\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{2\beta}}\lVert Z^{\beta}\boldsymbol{P}\hat{f}\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}}\lesssim\nu^{2}\lVert\mu^{\delta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}}.

This estimate is the only one where we need to crucially use the factor
\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{2\beta}
in the definition of the energy functional, as we explain in more details after the proof (see Remark
4.6
). Before proving this lemma, we recall some basic properties of the higher order moment projections.

Lemma 4.5
.

Let
\Theta[\cdot],\Lambda[\cdot]
be defined as in (
2.8
)-(
2.9
). Then, there exists a universal constant
0<\delta<1
such that

(4.57)

\displaystyle|\Theta[g]|+|\Lambda[g]|

\displaystyle\lesssim\lVert\mu^{\delta}g\rVert_{L^{2}_{v}}

(4.58)

\displaystyle|\Theta[\mathcal{L}(g)]|+|\Lambda[\mathcal{L}(g)]|

\displaystyle\lesssim\lVert\mu^{\delta}g\rVert_{L^{2}_{v}}.

Proof.

The proof of (
4.57
) is a direct consequence of Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and the fact that
\left\langle v\right\rangle^{p}\mu^{q}\in L^{2}_{v}
for any
p\geq 0
and
q>0
. Similarly, to prove (
4.58
) notice that

(4.59)

|\Lambda_{i}[\mathcal{L}(g)]|=|\left\langle(|v|^{2}-(d+2))v_{i}\sqrt{\mu},\mathcal{L}g\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}|=|\left\langle\mathcal{L}(|v|^{2}v_{i}\sqrt{\mu}),g\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}|\lesssim\lVert\mu^{\delta}g\rVert_{L^{2}_{v}}

and analogous bounds holds for
\Theta
, whence proving (
4.58
).
∎

We are now ready to prove Lemma
4.4
. In the proof we again omit the
L^{2}_{v}
subscript in the norms.

Proof of Lemma
4.4
.

In
[
34
, Lemma 4.1]
the following inequality is obtained (for
\nu=1
)

(4.60)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\nu}\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\mathcal{M}+\tilde{c}_{\star}\frac{|k|^{2}}{\nu}|(\hat{\rho},\hat{\mathsf{e}},\hat{\mathsf{m}})|^{2}\lesssim\nu\left\lVert\mu^{\delta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\right\rVert^{2},

for some
\tilde{c}_{\star}
independent of
\nu,k
. For convenience of the reader, we present below the proof of this inequality. However first, having at hand (
4.60
), we prove (
4.56
). Indeed, since
|k|\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu
, observe that

(4.61)

|k|^{2}\geq\delta_{0}^{2\beta}\frac{|tk|^{2\beta}}{\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{2\beta}}|k|^{2},

for any
|\beta|\geq 0
. Appealing to Lemma
4.3
, we get

(4.62)

\displaystyle|k|^{2}|(\hat{\rho},\hat{\mathsf{e}},\hat{\mathsf{m}})|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{B+1}\sum_{0\leq|\beta|\leq B}\delta_{0}^{2\beta}|k|^{2}\frac{|tk|^{2\beta}}{\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{2\beta}}|(\hat{\rho},\hat{\mathsf{e}},\hat{\mathsf{m}})|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{200(B+1)}\sum_{0\leq|\beta|\leq B}\delta_{0}^{2\beta}\bigg(\frac{|k|^{2}}{\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{2\beta}}\lVert Z^{\beta}\widehat{\boldsymbol{P}f}\rVert^{2}-C_{1}\sum_{0\leq|\widetilde{\beta}|\leq|\beta|-1}\frac{|tk|^{2\widetilde{\beta}}}{\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{2\widetilde{\beta}}}|(\hat{\rho},\hat{\mathsf{e}},\hat{\mathsf{m}})|^{2}\bigg)

(4.63)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2(B+1)}\sum_{0\leq|\beta|\leq B}\delta_{0}^{2\beta}|k|^{2}\frac{|tk|^{2\beta}}{\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{2\beta}}|(\hat{\rho},\hat{\mathsf{e}},\hat{\mathsf{m}})|^{2}.

Since

(4.64)

\sum_{0\leq|\beta|\leq B}\delta_{0}^{2\beta}C_{1}\sum_{0\leq|\widetilde{\beta}|\leq|\beta|-1}\frac{|tk|^{2\widetilde{\beta}}}{\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{2\widetilde{\beta}}}|(\hat{\rho},\hat{\mathsf{e}},\hat{\mathsf{m}})|^{2}\leq\delta_{0}^{2}BC_{1}\sum_{0\leq|\widetilde{\beta}|\leq B-1}\delta_{0}^{2\widetilde{\beta}}\frac{|tk|^{2\widetilde{\beta}}}{\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{2\widetilde{\beta}}}|(\hat{\rho},\hat{\mathsf{e}},\hat{\mathsf{m}})|^{2},

for
\delta_{0}
sufficiently small we deduce that

(4.65)

|k|^{2}|(\hat{\rho},\hat{\mathsf{e}},\hat{\mathsf{m}})|^{2}\geq\frac{1}{200(B+1)}\sum_{0\leq|\beta|\leq B}\delta_{0}^{2\beta}\frac{|k|^{2}}{\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{2\beta}}\lVert Z^{\beta}\widehat{\boldsymbol{P}f}\rVert^{2}.

Combining the inequality above with (
4.60
), we prove (
4.56
) with
\tilde{c}:=\tilde{c}_{\star}\delta_{0}^{2\beta}/(200(B+1))
.

To prove (
4.60
), applying
\nabla_{x}
to (
2.7c
) and taking the Fourier transform of the resulting equation and of (
4.48
), we get

(4.66)

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}{\rm Re}(\Lambda

\displaystyle[(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}]\cdot\widehat{ik\mathsf{e}})+|k|^{2}|\hat{\mathsf{e}}|^{2}

(4.67)

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle C_{\mathsf{e}}\big(|k|^{2}|\hat{\mathsf{m}}||\Lambda[(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}]|+|k|^{2}|\Lambda[(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}]|^{2}

(4.68)

\displaystyle+|k||\Lambda[ik\cdot v(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}]||\hat{\mathsf{e}}|+\nu|k||\Lambda[\mathcal{L}((I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}]||\hat{\mathsf{e}}|\big)

(4.69)

\displaystyle:=\,

\displaystyle C_{\mathsf{e}}\big(\mathcal{I}^{\mathsf{e}}_{1}+\dots+\mathcal{I}^{\mathsf{e}}_{4}\big)

From Young’s inequality and Lemma
4.5
we get for some
\widetilde{C}_{j}
’s,

(4.70)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}^{\mathsf{e}}_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{b_{1}}{16C_{\mathsf{e}}}|k|^{2}|\hat{\mathsf{m}}|^{2}+\widetilde{C}_{1}|k|^{2}\lVert\mu^{\delta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\rVert^{2},

(4.71)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}^{\mathsf{e}}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{C}_{2}|k|^{2}\lVert\mu^{\delta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\rVert^{2},

(4.72)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}^{\mathsf{e}}_{3}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{|k|^{2}}{16C_{\mathsf{e}}}|\hat{\mathsf{e}}|^{2}+\widetilde{C}_{3}|k|^{2}\lVert\mu^{\delta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\rVert^{2},

(4.73)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}^{\mathsf{e}}_{4}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{|k|^{2}}{16C_{\mathsf{e}}}|\hat{\mathsf{e}}|^{2}+\widetilde{C}_{4}\nu^{2}\lVert\mu^{\delta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\rVert^{2}.

Since
|k|\lesssim\nu
, using the bounds above in (
4.66
) we obtain

(4.74)

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}{\rm Re}(\Lambda[(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}]\cdot ik\hat{\mathsf{e}})+\frac{1}{2}|k|^{2}|\hat{\mathsf{e}}|^{2}\leq\frac{b_{1}}{16}|k|^{2}|\hat{\mathsf{m}}|^{2}+\widetilde{C}\nu^{2}\lVert\mu^{\delta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\rVert^{2}.

Consider now the second term in
\mathcal{M}
.
From (
4.47
) and (
2.7b
), one obtains

(4.75)

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}

\displaystyle{\rm Re}\bigg(\big(\Theta[(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}]+2(\hat{\mathsf{e}}I)\big):\big((ik\hat{\mathsf{m}}+(ik\hat{\mathsf{m}})^{T})\big)\bigg)+|k\otimes\hat{\mathsf{m}}+(k\otimes\hat{\mathsf{m}})^{T}|^{2}

(4.76)

\displaystyle\lesssim\,

\displaystyle|k|^{2}(|\hat{\mathsf{e}}|+|\hat{\rho}|)(|\Theta[(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}]|+|\hat{\mathsf{e}}|)+|k|^{2}|\Theta[(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}]|(|\Theta[(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}]|+|\hat{\mathsf{e}}|)

(4.77)

\displaystyle+|k||\hat{\mathsf{m}}|\big(|\Theta[\mathcal{F}(v\cdot\nabla_{x}((I-\boldsymbol{P})f))]|+\nu\Theta[\mathcal{L}((I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f})]|\big).

Notice that

(4.78)

\displaystyle|k\otimes\hat{\mathsf{m}}+(k\otimes\hat{\mathsf{m}})^{T}|^{2}=\sum_{i,j=1}^{d}(k_{j}\hat{\mathsf{m}}_{i}+k_{i}\hat{\mathsf{m}}_{j})^{2}=(|k|^{2}|\hat{\mathsf{m}}|^{2}+|k\cdot\hat{\mathsf{m}}|^{2}).

Hence, using Young’s inequality and Lemma
4.5
, similarly to (
4.74
), there exists
C_{\mathsf{m}}
(independent of
\mathsf{m}
) such that

(4.79)

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}

\displaystyle{\rm Re}\bigg(\big(\Theta[(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}]+2(\hat{\mathsf{e}}I)\big):\big((ik\hat{\mathsf{m}}+(ik\hat{\mathsf{m}})^{T})\big)\bigg)+\frac{1}{2}|k|^{2}|\hat{\mathsf{m}}|^{2}

(4.80)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{b_{2}}{16}|\hat{\rho}|^{2}+C_{\mathsf{m}}|k|^{2}|\hat{\mathsf{e}}|^{2}+\widetilde{C}_{\mathsf{m}}\nu^{2}\lVert\mu^{\delta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\rVert^{2}.

Arguing analogously for the remaing mixed inner product, we infer there exists
C_{\rho}
(independent of
\rho
) such that

(4.81)

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}

\displaystyle\operatorname{Re}(\hat{\mathsf{m}}\cdot ik\hat{\rho})+\frac{|k|^{2}}{2}|\hat{\rho}|^{2}\leq C_{\rho}|k|^{2}(|\hat{\mathsf{m}}|^{2}+|\hat{\mathsf{e}}|^{2}).

In light of (
4.52
), choosing
b_{2}\ll b_{1}\ll 1
and combining (
4.74
), (
4.79
), (
4.81
) we prove the bound (
4.60
) for a suitable constant
\tilde{c}_{\star}
.
∎

Remark 4.6
.

In the proof of Lemma
4.4
, it is crucial to exploit the factor
\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{2\beta}
to recover dissipation for all
Z^{\beta}\boldsymbol{P}f
by using only the one available for
(\rho,\mathsf{m},\mathsf{e})
(corresponding to
\beta=0
). In fact, one can also define the mixed inner product for
(t\nabla_{x})^{\otimes\beta}(\rho,\mathsf{m},\mathsf{e})
and try to obtain an estimate as (
4.56
). However, for
|\beta|\geq 1
we have some dangerous error terms coming from
\nu\mathcal{L}(I-\boldsymbol{P})f
in (
4.47
)-(
4.48
), where the main errors are proportional to

(4.82)

\nu\lVert\mu^{\delta}Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})f\rVert_{L^{2}_{v}}^{2}.

For
\beta=0
this is fine since, by the standard
L^{2}_{v}
energy estimate, in the dissipation functional we have a term that scales as
\nu\lVert\left\langle v\right\rangle^{\gamma/2+s}(I-\boldsymbol{P})f\rVert_{L^{2}_{v}}^{2}
. On the other hand, the available dissipation on
Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})f
has at most a factor
\nu^{-1}|k|^{2}
in front, which is much smaller than
\nu
in the regime
|k|\ll\nu
. To overcome this difficulty, one possibility is to exploit the estimate

(4.83)

\lVert\mu^{\delta}Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})f\rVert_{L^{2}_{v}}^{2}\lesssim\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{2}\sum_{0\leq|\widetilde{\beta}|\leq|\beta|-1}\lVert\mu^{\delta}Z^{\widetilde{\beta}}(I-\boldsymbol{P})f\rVert_{L^{2}_{v}}^{2}.

The loss
\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{2}
can indeed be easily controlled if we divide by
\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{2\beta}
each mixed inner product related to
(t\nabla_{x})^{\otimes\beta}(\rho,\mathsf{m},\mathsf{e})
. Hence, dividing by
\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{2\beta}
seems to be necessary to handle frequencies
|k|\ll\nu
, where the effect of the phase mixing generated by the transport can be too weak with respect to the collisional effects.

4.2.2.
Bounds on the microscopic part

In the remains to control the microscopic quantities in
E^{T.d.}_{M,B}
. Recall that
(I-\boldsymbol{P})f
satisfies

(4.84)

{\partial}_{t}(I-\boldsymbol{P})f+v\cdot\nabla_{x}(I-\boldsymbol{P})f+\nu\mathcal{L}(I-\boldsymbol{P})f=\boldsymbol{P}(v\cdot\nabla_{x}f)-v\cdot\nabla_{x}\boldsymbol{P}f.

In the following lemma, we collect the bounds that are needed to recover the dissipation on the microscopic part of
f
, which is a key ingredient to prove (
2.15
).

Lemma 4.7
.

Let
M>2\max\{|q_{\gamma,s}|,|\gamma|/2+s\}
,
\beta\geq 0
and
\tilde{c}
be the constant in (
4.56
). There exists
0<\tilde{\delta}<1
such that the following inequalities holds true: for the unweighted vector fields of the full solution we have

(4.85)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{|k|}{\nu}\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}

\displaystyle\lVert Z^{\beta}\hat{f}\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}}+\tilde{c}|k|\mathcal{A}[Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}]\leq\,\tilde{\delta}\nu\sum_{|\tilde{\beta}|\leq|\beta|}\mathcal{A}[Z^{\tilde{\beta}}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}]

(4.86)

\displaystyle+C_{\tilde{\delta}}\frac{|k|^{2}}{\nu}\sum_{|\beta_{1}|\leq|\beta|-1}\lVert Z^{\beta_{1}}\boldsymbol{P}\hat{f}\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}}+C|k|\sum_{|\beta_{1}|\leq|\beta|-1}\mathcal{A}[Z^{\beta_{1}}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}].

For the unweighted vector fields of the microscopic part we get

(4.87)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\lVert Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}}+\tilde{c}\nu\mathcal{A}[Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}]\leq\,

\displaystyle C_{\tilde{\delta}}\frac{|k|^{2}}{\nu}\sum_{|\tilde{\beta}|\leq|\beta|}\lVert Z^{\tilde{\beta}}\boldsymbol{P}\hat{f}\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}}

(4.88)

\displaystyle+C(\nu+|k|)\sum_{|\beta_{1}|\leq|\beta|-1}\mathcal{A}[Z^{\beta_{1}}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}],

and

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}

\displaystyle\lVert Z^{\beta}\nabla_{v}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}}+\tilde{c}\nu\mathcal{A}[Z^{\beta}\nabla_{v}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}]\leq\,{C}\nu(\mathcal{A}[Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}]+\mathcal{A}[\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M}Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}])

(4.89)

\displaystyle+{C}\frac{|k|^{2}}{\nu}\sum_{|\tilde{\beta}|\leq|\beta|}\lVert Z^{\tilde{\beta}}\boldsymbol{P}\hat{f}\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}}+C(\nu+|k|)\sum_{|\beta_{1}|\leq|\beta|-1}\mathcal{A}[Z^{\beta_{1}}\nabla_{v}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}].

For the weighted vector fields of the microscopic part we obtain

(4.90)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}

\displaystyle\lVert\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M}Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}}+\tilde{c}\nu\mathcal{A}[\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M}Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}]\leq C_{\tilde{\delta}}\nu\mathcal{A}[Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}]

(4.91)

\displaystyle+C_{\tilde{\delta}}\frac{|k|^{2}}{\nu}\sum_{|\tilde{\beta}|\leq|\beta|}\lVert Z^{\tilde{\beta}}\boldsymbol{P}\hat{f}\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}}+C(\nu+|k|)\sum_{|\beta_{1}|\leq|\beta|-1}\mathcal{A}[Z^{\beta_{1}}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}],

and

(4.92)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}

\displaystyle\lVert\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}Z^{\beta}\nabla_{v}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}}+\tilde{c}\nu\mathcal{A}[\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}\nabla_{v}Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}]

(4.93)

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{\delta}\frac{|k|^{2}}{\nu}\lVert\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}}

(4.94)

\displaystyle\quad+C_{\tilde{\delta}}\nu(\mathcal{A}[Z^{\beta}\nabla_{v}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}]+\mathcal{A}[Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}]+\mathcal{A}[\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M}Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}])

(4.95)

\displaystyle\quad+C_{\tilde{\delta}}\frac{|k|^{2}}{\nu}\sum_{|\tilde{\beta}|\leq|\beta|}\lVert Z^{\tilde{\beta}}\boldsymbol{P}\hat{f}\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}}+C(\nu+|k|)\sum_{|\beta_{1}|\leq|\beta|-1}\mathcal{A}[Z^{\beta_{1}}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}].

Finally, for the microscopic mixed inner product we have

(4.96)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\nu}\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}

\displaystyle{\rm Re}\left\langle\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}Z^{\beta}(\nabla_{x}(I-\boldsymbol{P})f)_{k},\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}Z^{\beta}\nabla_{v}(I-\boldsymbol{P})_{k}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}

(4.97)

\displaystyle+\frac{|k|^{2}}{\nu}\lVert\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}}

(4.98)

\displaystyle\leq C\nu(\mathcal{A}[\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}Z^{\beta}\nabla_{v}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}]+\mathcal{A}[\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M}Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}])

(4.99)

\displaystyle\quad+C\frac{|k|^{2}}{\nu}\sum_{|\tilde{\beta}|\leq|\beta|}\lVert Z^{\tilde{\beta}}\boldsymbol{P}\hat{f}\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}}+C(\nu+|k|)\sum_{|\beta_{1}|\leq|\beta|-1}\mathcal{A}[Z^{\beta_{1}}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}].

Proof.

We omit the subscript
L^{2}_{v}
for convenience of notation. From (
2.1
)
we compute that

(4.100)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\lVert Z^{\beta}\hat{f}\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle+\nu\left\langle\mathcal{L}(Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}),Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\right\rangle=-\nu{\rm Re}\left\langle Z^{\beta}\mathcal{L}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f},Z^{\beta}\boldsymbol{P}\hat{f}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\nu{\rm Re}\left\langle[\mathcal{L},Z^{\beta}](I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f},Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\right\rangle.

Appealing to (
3.7
), since
(a-b)^{2}\geq a^{2}/2-2b^{2}
, we get

(4.101)

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{L}(Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}),Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\geq C_{1}\mathcal{A}[(I-\boldsymbol{P})Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}]

(4.102)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{C_{1}}{2}\mathcal{A}[Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}]-2C_{1}\mathcal{A}[\boldsymbol{P}(Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f})].

By the definition of
Z^{\beta}
and the fact that
\boldsymbol{P}((t\nabla_{x})^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f})=0
for any multi-index
\beta
, notice that

(4.103)

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{P}(Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f})

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{P}\bigg(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\beta_{1}|+|\beta_{2}|=|\beta|\\
|\beta_{1}|\leq|\beta|-1\end{subarray}}C_{\beta_{1},\beta_{2}}(itk)^{\beta_{1}}(\nabla_{v})^{\beta_{2}}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\bigg)

(4.104)

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{P}\bigg(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\beta_{1}|+|\beta_{2}|=|\beta|\\
|\beta_{1}|\leq|\beta|-1\end{subarray}}\sum_{|\beta_{3}|+|\beta_{4}|=|\beta_{1}|}C_{\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\beta_{3},\beta_{4}}(\nabla_{v})^{\beta_{2}+\beta_{4}}Z^{\beta_{3}}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\bigg).

Since we are doing the projection onto the kernel of
\mathcal{L}
, we can safely move all the
v
-derivatives and weights on
\sqrt{\mu}
. Thus

(4.105)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}[\boldsymbol{P}(Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f})]\lesssim\sum_{|\beta_{1}|\leq{|\beta|-1}}\mathcal{A}[Z^{\beta_{1}}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}].

From the commutation properties (
3.15
)-(
3.16
) and (
3.8
), we obtain

(4.106)

\big|\left\langle[\mathcal{L},Z^{\beta}](I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f},Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\right\rangle\big|\leq\frac{C_{1}}{200}\mathcal{A}[{Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}}]+C\sum_{|\beta_{1}|\leq|\beta|-1}\mathcal{A}[Z^{\beta_{1}}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}].

It remains to control the term involving the macroscopic part, where we crucially exploit the cancellation

(4.107)

\left\langle(t\nabla_{x})^{\beta}\mathcal{L}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f},(t\nabla_{x})^{\beta}\boldsymbol{P}\hat{f}\right\rangle=0

to write

(4.108)

\displaystyle\left\langle Z^{\beta}\mathcal{L}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f},Z^{\beta}\boldsymbol{P}\hat{f}\right\rangle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\beta_{1}|+|\beta_{2}|=|\beta|\\
|\beta_{1}|\leq|\beta|-1\end{subarray}}C_{\beta_{1},\beta_{2}}\left\langle\mathcal{L}[(itk)^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}],(itk)^{\beta_{1}}(\nabla_{v})^{\beta_{2}}\boldsymbol{P}\hat{f}\right\rangle

(4.109)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\beta_{1}|+|\beta_{2}|=|\beta|\\
|\beta_{1}|\leq|\beta|-1\end{subarray}}C_{\beta_{1},\beta_{2}}\left\langle(itk)^{\beta_{1}}(\nabla_{v})^{\beta_{2}}\mathcal{L}[(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}],(itk)^{\beta}\boldsymbol{P}\hat{f}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\beta_{1}|+|\beta_{2}|=|\beta|\\
|\beta_{3}|+|\beta_{4}|=|\beta|\\
|\beta_{1}|\leq|\beta|-1,\,|\beta_{3}|\leq|\beta|-1\end{subarray}}C^{\beta_{1},\beta_{2}}_{\beta_{3},\beta_{4}}\left\langle(itk)^{\beta_{1}}(\nabla_{v})^{\beta_{2}}\mathcal{L}[(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}],(itk)^{\beta_{3}}(\nabla_{v})^{\beta_{4}}\boldsymbol{P}\hat{f}\right\rangle.

Since
(t\nabla_{x})^{\beta}=(Z-\nabla_{v})^{\beta}
, moving all the
v
-derivatives on the Maxwellian, it is not hard to show that

\big|\left\langle\mathcal{L}[(itk)^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}],(itk)^{\beta_{1}}(\nabla_{v})^{\beta_{2}}\boldsymbol{P}\hat{f}\right\rangle\big|\lesssim|tk|^{\beta_{1}}|(\hat{\rho},\hat{\mathsf{m}},\hat{\mathsf{e}})|\sum_{|\tilde{\beta}|\leq|\beta|}\sqrt{\mathcal{A}[Z^{\tilde{\beta}}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}]}

For the term in (
4.109
), we first move
(itk)^{\beta}
on
I-\boldsymbol{P}
and
(itk)^{\beta_{1}}(\nabla_{v})^{\beta_{2}}
on
\boldsymbol{P}
and then argue as above. The remaining terms are lower order and can be controlled analogously to finally obtain

(4.110)

\displaystyle\big|\left\langle Z^{\beta}\mathcal{L}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f},Z^{\beta}\boldsymbol{P}\hat{f}\right\rangle\big|\lesssim\,

\displaystyle\sqrt{\mathcal{A}[Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}]}\sum_{|\beta_{1}|\leq|\beta|-1}|tk|^{\beta_{1}}|(\hat{\rho},\hat{\mathsf{m}},\hat{\mathsf{e}})|

(4.111)

\displaystyle+\sum_{|\beta_{1}|\leq|\beta|-1}\sqrt{\mathcal{A}[Z^{\beta_{1}}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}]}|tk|^{\beta_{1}}|(\hat{\rho},\hat{\mathsf{m}},\hat{\mathsf{e}})|.

Combining (
4.100
), (
4.102
), (
4.105
), (
4.106
) and (
4.110
) we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{|k|}{\nu}\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}

\displaystyle\lVert Z^{\beta}\hat{f}\rVert^{2}+|k|\frac{C_{1}}{100}\mathcal{A}[Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}]\lesssim|k|\sqrt{\mathcal{A}[Z^{\beta_{1}}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}]}\sum_{|\beta_{1}|\leq|\beta|-1}|tk|^{\beta_{1}}|(\hat{\rho},\hat{\mathsf{m}},\hat{\mathsf{e}})|

(4.112)

\displaystyle+|k|\sum_{|\beta_{1}|\leq|\beta|-1}\sqrt{\mathcal{A}[Z^{\beta_{1}}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}]}|tk|^{\beta_{1}}|(\hat{\rho},\hat{\mathsf{m}},\hat{\mathsf{e}})|+|k|\sum_{|\beta_{1}|\leq|\beta|-1}\mathcal{A}[Z^{\beta_{1}}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}].

From Young’s inequality, for any
\tilde{\delta}>0
we have

(4.113)

\displaystyle|k|

\displaystyle\sqrt{\mathcal{A}[Z^{\beta_{1}}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}]}\sum_{|\beta_{1}|\leq|\beta|-1}|(tk)^{\beta_{1}}(\hat{\rho},\hat{\mathsf{m}},\hat{\mathsf{e}})|\leq\,\tilde{\delta}\nu\mathcal{A}[Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}]

\displaystyle\quad+\tilde{\delta}^{-1}\frac{|k|^{2}}{\nu}\sum_{|\beta_{1}|\leq|\beta|-1}|tk|^{2\beta_{1}}|(\hat{\rho},\hat{\mathsf{m}},\hat{\mathsf{e}})|^{2}.

Arguing analogously for the remaining terms and taking into account Lemma
4.3
, we prove (
4.85
).

To prove (
4.87
), from (
4.84
) we compute

(4.114)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}

\displaystyle\lVert Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\rVert^{2}+\nu\left\langle\mathcal{L}(Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}),Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\right\rangle

(4.115)

\displaystyle={\rm Re}\left\langle Z^{\beta}\boldsymbol{P}(ik\cdot v\hat{f}),Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\right\rangle-{\rm Re}\left\langle Z^{\beta}(ik\cdot v\boldsymbol{P}\hat{f}),Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\right\rangle

(4.116)

\displaystyle\qquad+\nu\left\langle[\mathcal{L},Z^{\beta}](I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f},Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\right\rangle.

For the last term in (
4.115
), using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and standard properties of the Maxwellian we get

(4.117)

\displaystyle\big|\left\langle Z^{\beta}(ik\cdot v\boldsymbol{P}\hat{f}),Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\right\rangle\big|\leq\,

\displaystyle\tilde{\delta}\nu\mathcal{A}[Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}]

(4.118)

\displaystyle+\tilde{\delta}^{-1}\frac{|k|^{2}}{\nu}\sum_{|\tilde{\beta}|\leq|\beta|}|tk|^{2\tilde{\beta}}|(\hat{\rho},\hat{\mathsf{m}},\hat{\mathsf{e}})|^{2}.

Notice that here we pay a large constant in front of the macroscopic dissipation up to order
|\beta|
. This is why we have to choose
c_{1}\ll c_{0}
in the definition of the energy functional (
2.10
).

To control the first term on the right-hand side of (
4.115
), we can again exploit a cancellation as in (
4.107
) to remove the highest order
t\nabla_{x}
-derivatives. In particular, we have

(4.119)

\left\langle(tik)^{\beta}\boldsymbol{P}(ik\cdot v\hat{f}),(tik)^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\right\rangle=0.

Hence, writing
(ik\cdot v\hat{f})=(ik\cdot v\boldsymbol{P}\hat{f})+(ik\cdot v(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f})
, and arguing as done in (
4.108
) to get (
4.110
), we infer

(4.120)

\displaystyle\big|

\displaystyle\left\langle Z^{\beta}\boldsymbol{P}(ik\cdot v\hat{f}),Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\right\rangle\big|\leq\,\tilde{\delta}(\nu+|k|)\mathcal{A}[Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}]

(4.121)

\displaystyle+C_{\tilde{\delta}}\frac{|k|^{2}}{\nu}\sum_{|\beta_{1}|\leq|\beta|-1}|tk|^{2\beta_{1}}|(\hat{\rho},\hat{\mathsf{m}},\hat{\mathsf{e}})|^{2}+C|k|\sum_{|\beta_{1}|\leq|\beta|-1}\mathcal{A}[Z^{\beta_{1}}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}].

Therefore, from the energy identity (
4.114
) we first combine (
4.102
), (
4.105
), (
4.106
), (
4.117
) and (
4.120
). Then, appealing to Lemma
4.3
and using that
|k|\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu
we prove (
4.87
) upon choosing
\tilde{\delta}
in (
4.117
) and (
4.120
) sufficiently small.

To get (
4.90
), we can proceed as done to obtain (
4.87
) but we need to handle the commutator between the weight and
\mathcal{L}
. In particular, the energy identity has the same structure of (
4.114
) with the extra term

(4.122)

\nu\left\langle[\mathcal{L},\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M}]Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f},Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\right\rangle.

Appealing to (
3.17
), we get

\nu\big|\left\langle[\mathcal{L},\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M}]Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f},Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\right\rangle\big|\lesssim\nu\left\lVert\mu^{\delta}Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\right\rVert^{2}\lesssim\nu\mathcal{A}[Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}],

whence proving (
4.90
).

To handle the terms with the
v
-derivatives, observe that

\displaystyle{\partial}_{t}

\displaystyle\nabla_{v}(I-\boldsymbol{P})f+v\cdot\nabla_{x}\nabla_{v}(I-\boldsymbol{P})f+\nu\mathcal{L}\nabla_{v}(I-\boldsymbol{P})f

(4.123)

\displaystyle=-\nabla_{x}f+\nu[\mathcal{L},\nabla_{v}](I-\boldsymbol{P})f+\nabla_{v}\boldsymbol{P}(v\cdot\nabla_{x}f)-v\cdot\nabla_{x}\nabla_{v}\boldsymbol{P}f.

On the left-hand side we have exactly the same structure we had without
\nabla_{v}
. On the right-hand side of (
4.123
), the first three terms are different with respect to the case without
v
-derivatives. The term
\nabla_{x}f
is the most dangerous one. Notice that here we can always pay a large constant for error terms involving the dissipation without
v
-derivatives since we choose
2^{-\mathtt{C}_{1}}\ll 2^{-\mathtt{C}_{0}}
in the definition of the energy functional (
2.10
).

To prove (
4.89
) and (
4.92
), observe that by the commutation properties in Lemma
3.3
, for
j=0,1
we have

(4.124)

\displaystyle\nu\big|

\displaystyle\left\langle\left\langle v\right\rangle^{j(M+q_{\gamma,s})}Z^{\beta}[\mathcal{L},\nabla_{v}](I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f},\left\langle v\right\rangle^{j(M+q_{\gamma,s})}Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\right\rangle\big|

(4.125)

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{\delta}\nu\mathcal{A}[\left\langle v\right\rangle^{j(M+q_{\gamma,s})}Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}]+C_{\tilde{\delta}}\nu\sum_{|\tilde{\beta}|\leq|\beta|}\mathcal{A}[\left\langle v\right\rangle^{jM}Z^{\tilde{\beta}}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}],

where we also used that
q_{\gamma,s}\leq 0
to obtain the last terms inside the sum above.

For the error terms generated by
\nabla_{v}\boldsymbol{P}(v\cdot\nabla_{x}f)=\nabla_{v}\boldsymbol{P}(v\cdot\nabla_{x}\boldsymbol{P}f)+\nabla_{v}\boldsymbol{P}(v\cdot\nabla_{x}(I-\boldsymbol{P})f)
, moving all the
v
-derivatives and weights on the term containing
\boldsymbol{P}
, we get that

(4.126)

\displaystyle\big|

\displaystyle\left\langle\left\langle v\right\rangle^{j(M+q_{\gamma,s})}\nabla_{v}\boldsymbol{P}(ik\cdot v\hat{f}),\left\langle v\right\rangle^{j(M+q_{\gamma,s})}\nabla_{v}Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\right\rangle\big|

(4.127)

\displaystyle\lesssim(|k|+\nu)\mathcal{A}[Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}]+\frac{|k|^{2}}{\nu}\sum_{|\tilde{\beta}|\leq|\beta|}|tk|^{2\tilde{\beta}}|(\hat{\rho},\hat{\mathsf{m}},\hat{\mathsf{e}})|^{2}.

For the error terms arising form
-\nabla_{x}f
, we argue as follows

\displaystyle\big|

\displaystyle\left\langle\left\langle v\right\rangle^{j(M+q_{\gamma,s})}Z^{\beta}(ik\hat{f}),\left\langle v\right\rangle^{j(M+q_{\gamma,s})}Z^{\beta}\nabla_{v}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\right\rangle\big|\leq\,

(4.128)

\displaystyle\big|\left\langle\left\langle v\right\rangle^{j(M+q_{\gamma,s})}Z^{\beta}(ik\boldsymbol{P}\hat{f}),\left\langle v\right\rangle^{j(M+q_{\gamma,s})}Z^{\beta}\nabla_{v}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\right\rangle\big|

(4.129)

\displaystyle+\big|\left\langle\left\langle v\right\rangle^{j(M+q_{\gamma,s})}Z^{\beta}(ik(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}),\left\langle v\right\rangle^{j(M+q_{\gamma,s})}Z^{\beta}\nabla_{v}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\right\rangle\big|:=\mathcal{I}^{j}_{1}+\mathcal{I}^{j}_{2}

For
\mathcal{I}^{j}_{1}
, integrating by parts in
v
and using the properties of the Maxwellian we have

(4.130)

\mathcal{I}_{1}^{j}\leq\tilde{\delta}\nu\mathcal{A}[Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}]+C_{\tilde{\delta}}\frac{|k|^{2}}{\nu}\sum_{|\tilde{\beta}|\leq|\beta|}|tk|^{2\tilde{\beta}}|(\hat{\rho},\hat{\mathsf{m}},\hat{\mathsf{e}})|^{2}.

To handle
\mathcal{I}^{0}_{2}
, since
M>2|\gamma|/2+s
and
|k|\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu
, notice that

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{2}^{0}\leq\,

\displaystyle\tilde{\tilde{\delta}}\nu\lVert Z^{\beta}\nabla_{v}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\rVert_{L^{2}_{s+\gamma/2}}^{2}+C_{\tilde{\tilde{\delta}}}\nu\lVert Z^{\beta}\left\langle v\right\rangle^{|s+\gamma/2|}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\tilde{\delta}\nu\mathcal{A}[Z^{\beta}\nabla_{v}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}]+C_{\tilde{\delta}}\nu\mathcal{A}[Z^{\beta}\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}].

For
\mathcal{I}^{1}_{2}
, we proceed as done to control the mixed inner product in the enhanced dissipation regime, see (
4.40
). Namely, appealing to the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality we get

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}^{1}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=0}^{+\infty}|k|\left\lVert\mathbbm{1}_{\{2^{j}\leq\left\langle v\right\rangle\leq 2^{j+1}\}}\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\right\rVert\left\lVert\mathbbm{1}_{\{2^{j}\leq\left\langle v\right\rangle\leq 2^{j+1}\}}\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}Z^{\beta}\nabla_{v}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\right\rVert

(4.131)

\displaystyle:=\sum_{j=0}^{+\infty}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{j}

Combining the Young’s inequality, the Gagliardo-Nirenberg inequality and using
q_{\gamma,s}<\gamma/(2s)
, as in (
4.32
)-(
4.40
), we obtain that

(4.132)

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{+\infty}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{j}\leq\,

\displaystyle\tilde{\delta}\frac{|k|^{2}}{\nu}\left\lVert\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\right\rVert^{2}

(4.133)

\displaystyle+\nu\left(\tilde{\delta}\mathcal{A}[\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}\nabla_{v}Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}]+C_{\tilde{\delta}}\mathcal{A}[\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M}Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}]\right).

Hence, the proofs of (
4.89
) and (
4.92
) follows by computing the time derivatives and using the estimates (
4.124
)-(
4.133
) (upon choosing
\tilde{\delta}
sufficiently small).

Finally, to prove (
4.96
) notice that the good term on the left-hand side of (
4.96
) is given from the
-\nabla_{x}f
in (
4.123
) since
[\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}\nabla_{x},\boldsymbol{P}]=0
. Similarly to (
4.5
), the terms arising from the transport
v\cdot\nabla_{x}
cancel out. All the remaining error terms can be treated in a similar way to what we did to get (
4.89
)-(
4.92
). For instance, we control the following term as

\displaystyle\big|

\displaystyle\left\langle\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}\nabla_{v}Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f},\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}Z^{\beta}\mathcal{L}(ik(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f})\right\rangle\big|\leq

\displaystyle C\nu(\mathcal{A}[\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}\nabla_{v}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}]+\mathcal{A}[\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}]),

where we used
|k|\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu
and
q_{\gamma,s}<0
. The remaining error terms can be handled analogously.
∎

We are now ready to present the proof of the monotonicity estimate (
4.2
), whence concluding the proof of Proposition
4.1
.

Proof of (
4.2
).

Recall the definition of
E^{T.d.}_{M,N}
in (
2.10
) and
\mathcal{D}^{T.d.}_{M,N}
in (
2.16
). When computing the time-derivative of
E^{T.d.}_{M,N}
we neglect the negative terms on the left-hand side appearing from the time derivative of
\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{-2\beta}
. Up to the constant independent of
\mathtt{C}_{j}
in front of each term, the first term in (
2.16
) directly follows by the standard
L^{2}_{v}
-energy estimate for the Boltzmann equation while the others are a suitable linear combination of the good terms in Lemma
4.4
and Lemma
4.7
.
The error terms are absorbed thanks to the choice of the constants
c_{i}\ll c_{i+1}
and
1\ll\mathtt{C}_{0}\ll\mathtt{C}_{1}
. Finally,
\delta_{d}
can be chosen to scale as the smallest constant in
E^{T.d.}_{M,N}
, namely
\delta_{d}=c_{3}\tilde{c}/1000
upon choosing
\mathtt{C}_{0}
sufficiently large.
∎

4.3.
Decay estimates

In this section, we aim at proving the decay estimates in Theorem
2.4
. Thanks to the monotonicity estimates in Proposition (
4.1
), it is enough to reconstruct the energy functional from the available (anisotropic) dissipation. In the hard potential case
2s+\gamma\geq 0
, this is a relatively simple consequence of some interpolation inequalities. On the other hand, for soft potentials the dissipation degenerates for large velocities. To overcome this problem, the standard procedure (e.g.
[
19
,
30
,
59
]
) is to use the control on higher-order moments. Indeed, the monotonicity estimates (
4.1
) and (
4.2
) are true also for the functionals
E^{*}_{M+M^{\prime},B}
for any
M^{\prime}>0
. Recalling the definition of
\mathcal{E}_{M,B}
given in (
2.18
), i.e.

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{M,B}(t)=\mathbbm{1}_{\nu/|k|\leq\delta_{0}}E^{e.d.}_{M,B}(t)+\mathbbm{1}_{\nu/|k|>\delta_{0}}E^{T.d.}_{M,B}(t),

we want to prove the following.

Lemma 4.8
.

Let
\mathcal{E}_{M,B}
be the functional defined in (
2.18
) and define
\varpi=(|\gamma|(2-s)+2s|q_{\gamma,s}|)/(1+s)
. Then, for any
M^{\prime}>0
, there exists constants
c,C>0
such that for all
R\geq 1
,

(4.134)

\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\mathcal{E}_{M,B}(t)\leq-c\frac{\lambda_{\nu,k}}{R^{\varpi}}\mathcal{E}_{M,B}(t)+C\frac{\lambda_{\nu,k}}{R^{|\gamma+2s|+2M^{\prime}}}\mathcal{E}_{M+M^{\prime},B}(0),

where
\lambda_{\nu,k}
is defined in (
1.19
).

Having at hand the inequality (
4.134
), whose proof we postpone at the end of this section, we are ready to prove Theorem
2.4
.

Proof of Theorem
2.4
.

For
0\leq t\leq 1
we have nothing to prove, therefore we assume
t>1
in the sequel. For simplicity of notation, we write
\lambda
instead of
\lambda_{\nu,k}
.
Let
0<p<1
to and choose
R
in (
4.134
) as

(4.135)

R=\left\langle\lambda t\right\rangle^{\frac{p}{\varpi}}.

Then, calling
\delta_{p}=c(1-p)/2
,
combining the inequality (
4.134
) with the choice of
R
we get

(4.136)

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\left({\rm e}^{\delta_{p}(\lambda t)^{1-p}}\mathcal{E}_{M,B}(t)\right)\lesssim\frac{\lambda{\rm e}^{\delta_{p}(\lambda t)^{1-p}}}{\left\langle\lambda t\right\rangle^{\frac{p(|\gamma+2s|+2M^{\prime})}{\varpi}}}\mathcal{E}_{M+M^{\prime},B}(0).

Define

(4.137)

M^{\prime\prime}=\frac{p(|\gamma+2s|+2M^{\prime})}{\varpi},

Choosing
M^{\prime}
sufficiently large, we have
M^{\prime\prime}>2
. Integrating in time (
4.136
), we get

(4.138)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{M,B}(t)\lesssim{\rm e}^{-\delta_{p}(\lambda t)^{1-p}}\mathcal{E}_{M,B}(0)+\mathcal{E}_{M+M^{\prime},B}(0){\rm e}^{-\delta_{p}(\lambda t)^{1-p}}\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\lambda{\rm e}^{\delta_{p}(\lambda\tau)^{1-p}}}{\left\langle\lambda\tau\right\rangle^{M^{\prime\prime}}}{\rm d}\tau.

To control the last integral in the inequality above, first observe that

(4.139)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\lambda{\rm e}^{\delta_{p}(\lambda\tau)^{1-p}}}{\left\langle\lambda\tau\right\rangle^{M^{\prime\prime}}}{\rm d}\tau

\displaystyle=\left(\int_{0}^{\lambda t/2}+\int_{\lambda t/2}^{\lambda t}\right)\frac{{\rm e}^{\delta_{p}s^{1-p}}}{\left\langle s\right\rangle^{M^{\prime\prime}}}{\rm d}s\lesssim{\rm e}^{\delta_{p}(\lambda t/2)^{1-p}}+\frac{1}{\left\langle\lambda t/2\right\rangle^{M^{\prime\prime}}}\int_{\lambda t/2}^{\lambda t}{\rm e}^{\delta_{p}s^{1-p}}{\rm d}s.

Having that

(4.140)

\displaystyle\int_{\lambda t/2}^{\lambda t}{\rm e}^{\delta_{p}s^{1-p}}{\rm d}s=\int_{\lambda t/2}^{\lambda t}s^{p}(s^{-p}{\rm e}^{\delta_{p}s^{1-p}}){\rm d}s\lesssim(\lambda t)^{p}{\rm e}^{\delta_{p}(\lambda t)^{1-p}},

we obtain

(4.141)

{\rm e}^{-\delta_{p}(\lambda t)^{1-p}}\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\lambda e^{c(\lambda\tau)^{1-p}}}{\left\langle\lambda\tau\right\rangle^{M^{\prime\prime}}}{\rm d}\tau\lesssim{\rm e}^{-\delta_{p}((\lambda t)^{1-p}-(\lambda t/2)^{1-p})}+\frac{(\lambda t)^{p}}{\left\langle\lambda t/2\right\rangle^{M^{\prime\prime}}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\left\langle\lambda t\right\rangle^{\widetilde{M}}},

where
\widetilde{M}=M^{\prime\prime}-p>1
and in the last inequality we used that
0<p<1
to bound the exponential term with the polynomially decaying one. Therefore, combining the bound above with (
4.138
), we finally get

(4.142)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{M,B}(t)\lesssim{\rm e}^{-\delta_{p}(\lambda t)^{1-p}}\mathcal{E}_{M,B}(0)+\frac{1}{\left\langle\lambda t\right\rangle^{\widetilde{M}}}\mathcal{E}_{M+M^{\prime},B}(0)

whence proving (
2.20
).
∎

It thus remain to prove Lemma
4.8
.

Proof of Lemma
4.8
.

From the definition of
\mathcal{E}_{M,B}(t)
(
2.18
) and
\mathcal{D}_{M,B}(t)
(
2.19
), we study the enhanced dissipation regime (
\nu/|k|\leq\delta_{0}
) and the Taylor dispersion one (
\nu/|k|>\delta_{0}
) separately.

\diamond

Taylor dispersion regime.
When
\nu/|k|>\delta_{0}
, recall the definitions of the energy
E^{T.d.}_{M,N}
and dissipation
\mathcal{D}^{T.d.}_{M,N}
given in (
2.10
) and (
2.16
) respectively. We need to reconstruct the energy functional from the available dissipation. Thanks to the equivalence (
2.13
), it is enough to reconstruct only the positive terms in
E^{T.d.}_{M,B}
.

Exploiting the integrability properties of the Maxwellian and
M+q_{\gamma,s}>1
, we get

(4.143)

\displaystyle\frac{|k|^{2}}{\nu}\big(

\displaystyle\lVert Z^{\beta}\boldsymbol{P}\hat{f}\rVert^{2}+\lVert\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\rVert^{2}\big)\gtrsim|k|(\nu^{-1}|k|\lVert Z^{\beta}\hat{f}\rVert^{2})

(4.144)

\displaystyle+\frac{|k|^{2}}{\nu}\big(\mathbbm{1}_{\beta=0}\lVert\hat{f}\rVert^{2}+\lVert Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\rVert^{2}+\lVert\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\rVert^{2}).

Next, first note the following

(4.145)

\left\lVert\chi_{|v|\leq R}g\right\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}\geq\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}-\left\lVert\chi_{|v|>R}g\right\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}\geq\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}-R^{-2M^{\prime}}\lVert\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M^{\prime}}g\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2},

for any
M^{\prime}_{\star}>0
.
To recover the weighted term without
v
-derivatives in
E^{T.d.}_{M,B}
, we use the last term in (
2.16
). Namely, in view of Proposition
3.1
, since
\nu\gtrsim|k|^{2}/\nu
, we have

\displaystyle\nu\mathcal{A}[\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M}Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}]\gtrsim\frac{|k|^{2}}{\nu}\lVert\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M}\mathbbm{1}_{|v|\leq R}Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2+s}}

(4.146)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\gtrsim\frac{\nu^{-1}|k|^{2}}{R^{|\gamma+2s|}}\big(\lVert\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M}Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\rVert^{2}-\lVert\mathbbm{1}_{|v|>R}\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M}Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\rVert^{2}\big)

(4.147)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\gtrsim\frac{\nu^{-1}|k|^{2}}{R^{|\gamma+2s|}}\bigg(\lVert\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M}Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\rVert^{2}-\frac{1}{R^{2M^{\prime}}}E^{T.d.}_{M+M^{\prime},B}(0)\bigg).

In the last inequality we used that
E^{T.d}_{M,N}
, thanks to Proposition
4.1
, is non-increasing for any
M
and (
4.145
).
Analogously, we reconstruct the piece of
E^{T.d.}_{M,N}
involving
v
-derivatives as follows

(4.148)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}[\left\langle v\right\rangle^{j(M+q_{\gamma,s})}\nabla_{v}Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}]\gtrsim\left\lVert\mathbbm{1}_{|v|\leq R}\left\langle v\right\rangle^{j(M+q_{\gamma,s})}\nabla_{v}Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\right\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2+s}}

(4.149)

\displaystyle\gtrsim\frac{1}{R^{|\gamma+2s|}}\left(\left\lVert\left\langle v\right\rangle^{j(M+q_{\gamma,s})}\nabla_{v}Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\right\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}}-\frac{1}{R^{2M^{\prime}}}E^{T.d.}_{M+M^{\prime},B}(0)\right).

Combining (
2.13
), (
4.143
), (
4.3
) and (
4.149
), since
|k|\gtrsim\nu^{-1}|k|^{2}
, we get

(4.150)

\mathcal{D}^{T.d.}_{M,B}(t)\gtrsim\frac{\nu^{-1}|k|^{2}}{R^{|\gamma+2s|}}E^{T.d.}_{M,B}(t)-\frac{\nu^{-1}|k|^{2}}{R^{|\gamma+2s|+2M^{\prime}}}E^{T.d.}_{M+M^{\prime},B}(0).

Therefore

(4.151)

\frac{1}{2}\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}E^{T.d}_{M,B}(t)\lesssim-\frac{\nu^{-1}|k|^{2}}{R^{|\gamma+2s|}}E^{T.d.}_{M,B}(t)+\frac{\nu^{-1}|k|^{2}}{R^{|\gamma+2s|+2M^{\prime}}}E^{T.d.}_{M+M^{\prime},B}(0).

In light of the definition of
\lambda_{\nu,k}
(
1.19
) and
\mathcal{E}_{M,B}
(
2.18
), since
|\gamma+2s|\leq\varpi
the bound (
4.134
) is proved in the Taylor dispersion regime.

\diamond

Enhanced dissipation regime.

The idea of proof is very similar to the previous one, we only need to be more careful with the right scaling of the
(\nu,k)
-dependent coefficients when reconstructing the energy functional
E^{e.d.}_{M,B}
(
2.1
) from the dissipation
\mathcal{D}^{e.d.}_{M,N}
(
2.5
).
In this case, recall that

(4.152)

E^{e.d.}_{M,B}(t)\approx\sum_{\alpha+|\beta|\leq B}\frac{2^{-\mathtt{C}\beta}\left\langle k\right\rangle^{\alpha}}{\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{2\beta}}\left(\lVert\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M}Z^{\beta}\hat{f}\rVert^{2}+a_{\nu,k}\lVert\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}Z^{\beta}\nabla_{v}\hat{f}\rVert^{2}\right).

To reconstruct the term without
v
-derivatives, we cannot proceed as in (
4.3
) since for large
|k|
’s we have
\nu\ll\lambda_{\nu,k}
. Thus, we have to exploit the good term generated by the mixed inner product. Namely, in view of Proposition
4.1
we know that
E^{e.d.}_{M,B}
is non-increasing for any
M
. Thus

(4.153)

\displaystyle b_{\nu,k}|k|^{2}\left\lVert\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}Z^{\beta}\hat{f}\right\rVert^{2}\gtrsim\frac{\lambda_{\nu,k}}{R^{2|q_{\gamma,s}|}}\left(\left\lVert\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M}Z^{\beta}\hat{f}\right\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}}-\frac{1}{R^{2M^{\prime}}}E^{e.d.}_{M+M^{\prime},N}(0)\right)

where we recall that in this case

(4.154)

\lambda_{\nu,k}=\frac{\delta_{1}}{b_{0}}|k|^{2}b_{\nu,k}=\delta_{1}\nu^{\frac{1}{1+2s}}|k|^{\frac{2s}{1+2s}}.

To reconstruct the piece with
v
-derivatives, we need to exploit an interpolation inequality. Combining Proposition
3.1
with Lemma
A.1
, we have

(4.155)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}[\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}\nabla_{v}Z^{\beta}\hat{f}]\gtrsim\frac{1}{R^{|\gamma|(2-s)}}\left\lVert\chi_{|v|\leq R}\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}\nabla_{v}Z^{\beta}\hat{f}\right\rVert^{2}_{H^{s}}.

From the definitions of
\lambda_{\nu,k}
(
1.19
) and
a_{\nu,k}
(
2.4
), notice that

(4.156)

\lambda_{\nu,k}a_{\nu,k}=\delta_{1}^{\frac{1}{1+s}}a_{0}^{\frac{s}{1+s}}(\lambda_{\nu,k})^{\frac{s}{1+s}}(\nu a_{\nu,k})^{\frac{1}{1+s}}

Therefore, using the Gagliardo-Nirenberg inequality we have

(4.157)

\displaystyle\lambda_{\nu,k}a_{\nu,k}\left\lVert\nabla_{v}g\right\rVert_{L^{2}(B_{R})}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta_{1}^{\frac{1}{1+s}}a_{0}^{\frac{s}{1+s}}(\nu a_{\nu,k}\left\lVert\nabla_{v}g\right\rVert_{H^{s}(B_{R})}^{2})^{\frac{1}{1+s}}(\lambda_{\nu,k}\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{2}(B_{R})}^{2})^{\frac{s}{1+s}}

(4.158)

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta_{1}^{\frac{1}{1+s}}a_{0}^{\frac{s}{1+s}}\left(\nu a_{\nu,k}\left\lVert\nabla_{v}g\right\rVert_{H^{s}(B_{R})}^{2}+\lambda_{\nu,k}\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{2}(B_{R})}^{2}\right).

Since
a_{0},\delta_{1}\ll 1
, combining (
4.153
), (
4.155
) with (
4.157
), we infer

(4.159)

\displaystyle\nu a_{\nu,k}

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}[\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}\nabla_{v}Z^{\beta}\hat{f}]+b_{\nu,k}|k|^{2}\left\lVert\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}Z^{\beta}\hat{f}\right\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}}

(4.160)

\displaystyle\gtrsim\frac{\nu a_{\nu,k}}{R^{|\gamma|(2-s)}}\left\lVert\chi_{|v|\leq R}\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}\nabla_{v}Z^{\beta}\hat{f}\right\rVert^{2}_{H^{s}}+\frac{\lambda_{\nu,k}}{R^{2|q_{\gamma,s}|}}\left\lVert\chi_{|v|\leq R}\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M}Z^{\beta}\hat{f}\right\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}}

(4.161)

\displaystyle\gtrsim\lambda_{\nu,k}\frac{a_{\nu,k}}{R^{\varpi}}\left\lVert\chi_{|v|\leq R}\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}\nabla_{v}Z^{\beta}\hat{f}\right\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}}

(4.162)

\displaystyle\gtrsim\frac{\lambda_{\nu,k}}{R^{\varpi}}\left(a_{\nu,k}\left\lVert\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}\nabla_{v}Z^{\beta}\hat{f}\right\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}}-\frac{1}{R^{2M^{\prime}}}E^{e.d.}_{M+M^{\prime},B}(0)\right)

where we also used
\varpi=(|\gamma|(2-s)+2s|q_{\gamma,s}|)/(1+s)
, the inequality (
4.145
) and the fact that
E^{e.d.}_{M,B}
is non-increasing for any
M
.
Hence, recalling the definition of
\mathcal{D}^{e.d.}_{M,N}
, combining (
4.153
), (
4.159
) and (
4.152
), we obtain

(4.163)

\mathcal{D}^{e.d.}_{M,B}\gtrsim\frac{\lambda_{\nu,k}}{R^{\varpi}}E^{e.d.}_{M,B}-\frac{\lambda_{\nu,k}}{R^{\varpi+2M^{\prime}}}E^{e.d.}_{M+M^{\prime},B}(0).

Consequently, appealing to (
4.44
) we get

(4.164)

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}E^{e.d.}_{M,B}\leq-c\frac{\lambda_{\nu,k}}{R^{\varpi}}E^{e.d.}_{M,B}+C\frac{\lambda_{\nu,k}}{R^{\varpi+2M^{\prime}}}E^{e.d.}_{M+M^{\prime},N}(0),

whence proving (
4.134
) in the enhanced dissipation regime since
|\gamma+2s|\leq\varpi
.

∎

5.
Nonlinear estimates

In this section we aim at proving Proposition
2.8
, which requires the control of several error terms arising from the time derivative of the energy functionals. We first define such error terms and we collect their bounds Propositions
5.2
-
5.7
. Having at hand the aforementioned bounds, we finally prove Proposition
2.8
.
Recall that

(5.1)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{\left|k\right|\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle^{2J}E^{T.d.}_{M,B}(t,k){\rm d}k+\int_{\left|k\right|>\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle^{2J}E^{e.d.}_{M,B}(t,k){\rm d}k,

(5.2)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{LF}(t)

\displaystyle=\sup_{k:\left|k\right|\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle^{2J^{\prime}}E^{T.d.}_{M^{\prime},B^{\prime}}(t,k)+\sup_{k:\left|k\right|>\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle^{2J^{\prime}}E^{e.d.}_{M^{\prime},B^{\prime}}(t,k),

(5.3)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{mom}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{\left|k\right|\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}E^{T.d.}_{M+M_{J},B}(t,k){\rm d}k+\int_{\left|k\right|>\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}E^{e.d.}_{M+M_{J},B}(t,k){\rm d}k,

(5.4)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{mom,LF}(t)

\displaystyle=\sup_{k:\left|k\right|\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}E^{T.d.}_{M^{\prime}+M_{J^{\prime}},B^{\prime}}(t,k)+\sup_{k:\left|k\right|>\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}E^{e.d.}_{M^{\prime}+M_{J^{\prime}},B^{\prime}}(t,k),

where we assume the conditions (
2.23
). Associated to these energies, we have the dissipations defined as above with

(5.5)

(\mathcal{E}_{*},E^{T.d.}_{*},E^{e.d.}_{*})\to(\mathcal{D}_{*},\mathcal{D}^{T.d.}_{*},\mathcal{D}^{e.d.}_{*})

Taking the time derivative of
\mathcal{E}
and exploiting the pointwise in frequency monotonicity estimates in Proposition
4.1
, we know that

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\mathcal{E}\leq\,

\displaystyle-\delta_{\star}\mathcal{D}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}L_{J,M,B}{\rm d}k

(5.6)

\displaystyle+\nu\int_{\left|k\right|\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle^{2J}NL^{T.d.}_{M,B}{\rm d}k+\nu\int_{\left|k\right|>\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle^{2J}NL^{e.d.}_{M,B}{\rm d}k,

where
\delta_{\star}=\min\{\delta_{e},\delta_{d}\}>0
with
\delta_{e},\delta_{d}
being the one appearing in (
4.1
)-(
4.2
). The linear error term is

(5.7)

L_{J,M,B}:=2J\lambda_{\nu,k}\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle^{2J-1}(\mathbbm{1}_{\left|k\right|\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}E^{T.d}_{M,B}+\mathbbm{1}_{\left|k\right|>\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}E^{e.d.}_{M,B}).

We define the nonlinear error terms as follows: the one arising from the enhanced dissipation energy functional
E^{e.d.}_{M,B}
is

(5.8)

\displaystyle NL^{e.d.}_{M,B}=\sum_{\alpha+|\beta|\leq B}

\displaystyle\frac{2^{-\mathtt{C}\beta}}{\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{2\beta}}\left\langle k\right\rangle^{2\alpha}\bigg(\bigg|\left\langle\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M}Z^{\beta}\widehat{\Gamma(f,f)},\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M}Z^{\beta}\hat{f}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\bigg|

(5.9)

\displaystyle+a_{\nu,k}\bigg|\left\langle\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}\nabla_{v}Z^{\beta}\widehat{\Gamma(f,f)},\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}\nabla_{v}Z^{\beta}\hat{f}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\bigg|

(5.10)

\displaystyle+b_{\nu,k}\bigg|\left\langle\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}Z^{\beta}\widehat{ik\Gamma(f,f)},\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}\nabla_{v}Z^{\beta}\hat{f}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\bigg|

(5.11)

\displaystyle+b_{\nu,k}\bigg|\left\langle\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}\nabla_{v}Z^{\beta}\widehat{\Gamma(f,f)},\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}Z^{\beta}ik\hat{f}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\bigg|\bigg).

From
E^{T.d.}_{M,B}
, using that
\langle{\Gamma(f,f),f}\rangle=\langle{\Gamma(f,f),(I-\boldsymbol{P})f}\rangle
, we get

(5.12)

\displaystyle NL^{T.d.}_{M,B}=\bigg|\left\langle\widehat{\Gamma(f,f)},(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\bigg|+\sum_{\alpha+|\beta|\leq B}\sum_{j=0}^{1}\frac{2^{-\mathtt{C}_{j}\beta}}{\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{2\beta}}\bigg(\bigg|\frac{|k|}{\nu}\left\langle Z^{\beta}\widehat{\Gamma(f,f)},Z^{\beta}\hat{f}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\bigg|

(5.13)

\displaystyle\qquad+c_{1}\bigg|\left\langle Z^{\beta}(\nabla_{v})^{j}\widehat{\Gamma(f,f)},Z^{\beta}(\nabla_{v})^{j}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f})\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\bigg|

(5.14)

\displaystyle\qquad+c_{2}\bigg|\left\langle\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+jq_{\gamma,s}}Z^{\beta}(\nabla_{v})^{j}\widehat{\Gamma(f,f)},\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+jq_{\gamma,s}}Z^{\beta}(\nabla_{v})^{j}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\bigg|\bigg)

(5.15)

\displaystyle+\frac{c_{0}}{\nu}\mathcal{M}_{\Gamma}+\sum_{\alpha+|\beta|\leq B}\frac{2^{-\mathtt{C}\beta}}{\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{2\beta}}c_{3}\bigg(\bigg|\left\langle\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}Z^{\beta}ik\widehat{\Gamma(f,f)},\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}\nabla_{v}Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\bigg|

(5.16)

\displaystyle\qquad+\bigg|\left\langle\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}\nabla_{v}Z^{\beta}\widehat{\Gamma(f,f)},\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})ik\hat{f}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\bigg|\bigg),

where

(5.17)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{\Gamma}:=\,

\displaystyle\big|\Lambda[\widehat{\Gamma(f,f)}]\cdot(ik\hat{\mathsf{e}})\big|+\big|\Theta[\widehat{\Gamma(f,f)}]:(ik\hat{\mathsf{m}}+(ik\hat{\mathsf{m}})^{T})\big|.

Observe that the term above, which is given by the mixed inner product with macroscopic variables defined in (
2.12
), contains only the nonlinear terms for the equations involving the higher-order moments
\Theta
and
\Lambda
. This is a consequence of
\left\langle\Gamma(f,f),\boldsymbol{P}f\right\rangle=0
.

The time derivative of
\mathcal{E}_{LF}
is as (
5.6
) with

(5.18)

\displaystyle(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{D})\to(\mathcal{E}_{LF},\mathcal{D}_{LF}),\quad(J,M,B)\to(J^{\prime},M^{\prime},B^{\prime})\quad\text{and}\quad\int\to\sup.

For
\mathcal{E}_{mom,*}
instead we change (
5.6
) as follows

(5.19)

\displaystyle(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{D})\to(\mathcal{E}_{mom},\mathcal{D}_{mom}),\quad(J,M,B)\to(0,M+M_{J},B),

(5.20)

\displaystyle(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{D})\to(\mathcal{E}_{mom,LF},\mathcal{D}_{mom,LF}),\quad(J,M,B)\to(0,M^{\prime}+M_{J^{\prime}},B)\quad\text{and}\quad\int\to\sup

Remark 5.1
.

Notice that, since we are not imposing any time-decay for
\mathcal{E}_{mom,*}
, we do not have the linear error term. Namely
L_{0,M,B}=0
.

The goal is then to bound the error terms in (
5.6
) (with the changes (
5.18
)-(
5.20
)) for
\mathcal{E}
(each) energy functional. The bounds for the linear errors are a direct consequence of the lower bounds on the dissipation functionals given in Section
4.3
. In particular, we show the following in Section
5.1
.

Proposition 5.2
.

Let
L_{J,M,B}
be defined as in (
5.7
). Then, there exists constants
C_{1},C_{2}>0
(explicitly computable) such that

(5.21)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}L_{J,M,B}{\rm d}t{\rm d}k\leq\frac{\delta_{\star}}{8}\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{D}{\rm d}t+C_{1}\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}(\mathcal{E}_{mom}(s)),

(5.22)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\sup_{k\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}L_{J^{\prime},M^{\prime},B^{\prime}}{\rm d}t\leq\frac{\delta_{\star}}{8}\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{D}_{LF}{\rm d}t+C_{2}\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}(\mathcal{E}_{mom,LF}(s)),

where
\delta_{\star}>0
is the constant in (
5.6
).

To state the bounds for the nonlinear error terms, in the following propositions we always consider
\mathcal{E}_{*},\mathcal{D}_{*}
to be the functionals defined in (
5.1
)-(
5.5
) and

NL^{e.d.}_{*}
,
NL^{T.d.}_{*}
the nonlinear errors defined in (
5.8
), (
5.12
) respectively. We control separately the nonlinear errors arising from the time derivative of each of the energy functionals in (
5.1
)-(
5.4
). For the ones associated with
\mathcal{E}
, in Section
5.2
we prove the following.

Proposition 5.3
.

The following inequality holds true

\displaystyle\nu\int_{\left|k\right|\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle^{2J}NL^{T.d.}_{M,B}{\rm d}k+\nu\int_{\left|k\right|>\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle^{2J}NL^{e.d.}_{M,B}{\rm d}k

(5.23)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\mathcal{E}}\sqrt{\mathcal{D}}\left(\sqrt{\mathcal{D}}+\big(\nu^{d}\mathbbm{1}_{t\leq\nu^{-1}}+\left\langle\nu/t\right\rangle^{\frac{d}{2}}\mathbbm{1}_{t>\nu^{-1}}\big)\sqrt{\mathcal{E}_{LF}}\right)(t).

To control the
L^{\infty}_{k}
energy functional
\mathcal{E}_{LF}
, which is needed in (
5.23
) and necessary to obtain the optimal time-decay rates, in Section
5.3
we show the following.

Proposition 5.4
.

The following inequality holds true

\displaystyle\nu\sup_{k:\left|k\right|\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle^{2J^{\prime}}NL^{T.d.}_{M^{\prime},B^{\prime}}+\nu\sup_{k:\left|k\right|>\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle^{2J^{\prime}}NL^{e.d.}_{M^{\prime},B^{\prime}}

(5.24)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\mathcal{D}_{LF}}\left(\sqrt{\mathcal{E}}\sqrt{\mathcal{D}}+\big(\nu^{d}\mathbbm{1}_{t\leq\nu^{-1}}+\left\langle\nu/t\right\rangle^{\frac{d}{2}}\mathbbm{1}_{t>\nu^{-1}}\big)\mathcal{E}_{LF}\right)(t).

Remark 5.5
.

As it will be clear from the proof of Proposition
5.4
, we are allowed to use
\mathcal{E}
in (
5.24
) because
\mathcal{E}_{LF}
is a lower order energy with respect to
\mathcal{E}
. Indeed, it requires less derivatives, decay and weights, a fact that is crucial to close the estimates.

We finally have to estimate the higher order moments functionals
\mathcal{E}_{mom,*}
, appearing in the bounds for the linear error terms (
5.21
)-(
5.22
). Recall that for the time derivative of
\mathcal{E}_{mom,*}
we only have nonlinear error terms, since
L_{0,M,B}=0
as observed in Remark
5.1
. Hence, to control the time derivative of
\mathcal{E}_{mom}
the following proposition is sufficient.

Proposition 5.6
.

The following inequality holds true

\displaystyle\nu\int_{\left|k\right|\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}NL^{T.d.}_{M+M_{J},B}{\rm d}k+\nu\int_{\left|k\right|>\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}NL^{e.d.}_{M+M_{J},B}{\rm d}k

(5.25)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\mathcal{E}_{mom}}\sqrt{\mathcal{D}_{mom}}\left(\sqrt{\mathcal{D}_{mom}}+\big(\nu^{d}\mathbbm{1}_{t\leq\nu^{-1}}+\left\langle\nu/t\right\rangle^{\frac{d}{2}}\mathbbm{1}_{t>\nu^{-1}}\big)\sqrt{\mathcal{E}_{LF}}\right)(t).

Similary, for the
L^{\infty}_{k}
functional
\mathcal{E}_{mom,LF}
we obtain the following.

Proposition 5.7
.

The following inequality holds true

(5.26)

\displaystyle\nu\sup_{k:\left|k\right|\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}NL^{T.d.}_{M^{\prime}+M_{J^{\prime}},B^{\prime}}+\nu\sup_{k:\left|k\right|>\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}NL^{e.d.}_{M^{\prime}+M_{J^{\prime}},B^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\mathcal{D}_{mom,LF}}\bigg((\sqrt{\mathcal{E}_{mom}}+\sqrt{\mathcal{E}})\sqrt{\mathcal{D}_{mom}}+\big(\nu^{d}\mathbbm{1}_{t\leq\nu^{-1}}+\left\langle\nu/t\right\rangle^{\frac{d}{2}}\mathbbm{1}_{t>\nu^{-1}}\big)\sqrt{\mathcal{E}_{LF}\mathcal{E}_{mom,LF}}\bigg)(t).

The proofs of Propositions
5.6
and
5.7
are given in Section
5.4
and are obtained via minor modifications in the arguments done to arrive at (
5.23
) and (
5.24
).

We first show how Propositions
5.2
-
5.7
imply the main bootstrap Proposition
2.8
.

Proof of Proposition
2.8
.

We begin by proving that under the bootstrap assumptions (
2.25
)-(
2.26
), the inequality (
2.24
) holds true with half the constant on the right-hand side of (
2.24
). Indeed, integrating in time (
5.6
) and using the bound (
5.21
) and (
5.23
), we have

(5.27)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(t)

\displaystyle+\frac{7}{8}\delta_{\star}\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{D}(\tau){\rm d}\tau\leq\mathcal{E}(0)+C_{1}\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}(\mathcal{E}_{mom}(s))

(5.28)

\displaystyle+C\int_{0}^{t}\bigg(\sqrt{\mathcal{E}}\mathcal{D}+\left(\nu^{d}\mathbbm{1}_{t\leq\nu^{-1}}+\left\langle\nu/\tau\right\rangle^{\frac{d}{2}}\mathbbm{1}_{t>\nu^{-1}}\right)\sqrt{\mathcal{E}_{LF}}\sqrt{\mathcal{E}}\sqrt{\mathcal{D}}\bigg)(\tau){\rm d}\tau.

From the bootstrap assumptions (
2.25
)-(
2.26
), for any
t\in[0,T]
we get

(5.29)

\displaystyle C_{1}\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}(\mathcal{E}_{mom}(s))\leq 4C_{1}\varepsilon^{2},

(5.30)

\displaystyle C\int_{0}^{t}(\sqrt{\mathcal{E}}\mathcal{D})(\tau){\rm d}\tau\leq\varepsilon\widetilde{C}\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{D}(\tau){\rm d}\tau,

\displaystyle C\int_{0}^{t}\left(\nu^{d}\mathbbm{1}_{\tau\leq\nu^{-1}}+\left\langle\nu/\tau\right\rangle^{\frac{d}{2}}\mathbbm{1}_{\tau>\nu^{-1}}\right)(\sqrt{\mathcal{E}_{LF}}\sqrt{\mathcal{E}}\sqrt{\mathcal{D}})(\tau){\rm d}\tau

(5.31)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\varepsilon^{3}\widetilde{C}_{1}\int_{0}^{t}\left(\nu^{d}\mathbbm{1}_{\tau\leq\nu^{-1}}+\left\langle\nu/\tau\right\rangle^{\frac{d}{2}}\mathbbm{1}_{\tau>\nu^{-1}}\right)^{2}{\rm d}\tau+\varepsilon\widetilde{C}_{2}\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{D}(\tau){\rm d}\tau.

Since
d\geq 2
, we know that the first term on the right-hand side of (
5.31
) is integrable in time. Thus, we obtain that

(5.32)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(t)+\big(\frac{7}{8}-\varepsilon(\widetilde{C}+\widetilde{C}_{2})\big)\delta_{\star}\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{D}(\tau){\rm d}\tau\leq(1+4C_{1}+\varepsilon\widetilde{C}_{1})\varepsilon^{2}.

Setting

(5.33)

\mathsf{B}_{1}:=2(1+4C_{1}),

and choosing
\varepsilon
small enough, we prove that (
2.24
) holds with half the constant on the right-hand side. The arguments for the other functionals are analogous and we omit their proof.
∎

The rest of this section is dedicated to the proof of Propositions
5.2
-
5.7
. In particular, for the nonlinear error terms we need to compare the solution in different frequency regimes, therefore we state some technical estimates used throughout the proof.

Lemma 5.8
.

Let
k,\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. For
a_{\nu,k}
defined in (
2.4
), we have

(5.34)

\mathbbm{1}_{|k|\gtrsim\nu}a_{\nu,k}\lesssim 1,\qquad\mathbbm{1}_{|k|\approx|\xi|\gtrsim\nu}\frac{a_{\nu,k}}{a_{\nu,\xi}}\lesssim 1,\qquad\mathbbm{1}_{|k|\approx|\xi|\gtrsim\nu}\frac{a_{\nu,k}}{a_{\nu,k-\xi}}\lesssim 1.

Let
\lambda_{\nu,k}
be defined as in (
1.19
). Then

(5.35)

\mathbbm{1}_{|k|\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle\lesssim\mathbbm{1}_{|\xi|\gtrsim\nu}\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,\xi}t\right\rangle.

For any
0<J^{\prime}\leq J-1
, the following inequality holds

(5.36)

\mathbbm{1}_{|k|\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle^{J^{\prime}}\lesssim\mathbbm{1}_{|\xi|\gtrsim\nu}\frac{1}{\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle}\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,\xi}t\right\rangle^{J}.

Let
E^{e.d.}_{M,B}
and
E^{T.d.}_{M,B}
be defined as in (
2.1
) and (
2.10
) respectively. Then

(5.37)

\displaystyle\mathbbm{1}_{|k|\sim\nu}E^{e.d.}_{M,B}(t,k)\approx\mathbbm{1}_{|k|\sim\nu}E^{T.d.}_{M,B}(t,k),

(5.38)

\displaystyle\mathbbm{1}_{|k|\sim\nu}\mathcal{D}^{e.d.}_{M,B}(t,k)\approx\mathbbm{1}_{|k|\sim\nu}\mathcal{D}^{T.d.}_{M,B}(t,k).

Proof.

The first and the second inequalities in (
5.34
) are immediate from the definition of
a_{\nu,k}
. For the last one, when
|k|\approx|\xi|\gtrsim\nu
, notice that

(5.39)

\frac{a_{\nu,k}}{a_{\nu,k-\xi}}=\left(\frac{|k-\xi|}{|k|}\right)^{\frac{2}{1+2s}}\lesssim 1.

To prove (
5.35
), from the expression of
\lambda_{\nu,k}
, it is enough to observe that

(5.40)

\mathbbm{1}_{|k|\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\frac{|k|^{2}}{\nu}\lesssim\mathbbm{1}_{|\xi|\gtrsim\nu}\nu^{\frac{1}{1+2s}}|\xi|^{\frac{2s}{1+2s}}.

To prove (
5.36
) notice that, whenever
|\xi|\gtrsim\nu
we get

(5.41)

\langle\lambda_{\nu,\xi}t\rangle\approx\langle\nu^{\frac{1}{1+2s}}|\xi|^{\frac{2s}{1+2s}}t\rangle\gtrsim\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle.

Hence

(5.42)

\mathbbm{1}_{|k|\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle^{J^{\prime}}\lesssim\mathbbm{1}_{|\xi|\gtrsim\nu}\frac{1}{\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,\xi}t\right\rangle^{J-J^{\prime}}}\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,\xi}t\right\rangle^{J}\lesssim\mathbbm{1}_{|\xi|\gtrsim\nu}\frac{1}{\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle}\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,\xi}t\right\rangle^{J},

which proves (
5.36
).

For the equivalence (
5.37
), by Cauchy-Schwartz inequality and the fact that

(5.43)

(b_{\nu,k}|k|)^{2}=\frac{b_{0}^{2}}{a_{0}}a_{\nu,k}\ll a_{\nu,k},

see (
4.42
), we get

(5.44)

\displaystyle E^{e.d.}_{M,B}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\alpha+|\beta|\leq B}\frac{\left\langle k\right\rangle^{\alpha}}{\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{2\beta}}\bigg(2\left\lVert\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M}Z^{\beta}\hat{f}\right\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}}+a_{\nu,k}(1+\frac{b_{0}^{2}}{a_{0}})\left\lVert\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}Z^{\beta}\nabla_{v}\hat{f}\right\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}}\bigg)

(5.45)

\displaystyle E^{e.d.}_{M,B}

\displaystyle\geq 2^{-\mathtt{C}B-2}\sum_{\alpha+|\beta|\leq B}\frac{\left\langle k\right\rangle^{\alpha}}{\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{2\beta}}\bigg(\left\lVert\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M}Z^{\beta}\hat{f}\right\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}}+2a_{\nu,k}(1-2\frac{b_{0}^{2}}{a_{0}})\left\lVert\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}Z^{\beta}\nabla_{v}\hat{f}\right\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}}\bigg).

Therefore, when
|k|\approx\nu
we deduce that

(5.46)

E^{e.d.}_{M,B}\approx\sum_{\alpha+|\beta|\leq B}\frac{1}{\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{2\beta}}\bigg(\left\lVert\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M}Z^{\beta}\hat{f}\right\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}}+\tilde{c}\left\lVert\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}Z^{\beta}\nabla_{v}\hat{f}\right\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}}\bigg),

for some
\tilde{c}
explicitly computable.

For
E^{T.d}_{M,B}
, recalling the definition of
\mathcal{M}
(
2.12
), we first notice that

(5.47)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\nu}|\mathcal{M}|\leq C_{m}\frac{|k|}{\nu}\lVert\hat{f}\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}}\leq C_{m}\delta_{0}^{-1}\lVert\hat{f}\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}},

for some constant
C_{m}>0
. Thus, there exists also another
\tilde{C}_{m}>0
such that

\displaystyle E^{T.d.}_{M,B}\leq(\frac{1}{2}+c_{0}\delta_{0}^{-1}C_{m})\lVert\hat{f}\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{|\beta|\leq B}\sum_{j=0}^{1}\frac{1}{\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{2\beta}}\bigg(\frac{|k|}{\nu}\lVert Z^{\beta}\hat{f}\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}}+(c_{1}+c_{3}\delta_{0}^{-1}\tilde{C}_{m})\lVert Z^{\beta}\nabla_{v}^{j}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}}

(5.48)

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt+(c_{2}+c_{3}\delta_{0}^{-1}\tilde{C}_{m})\lVert\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+jq_{\gamma,s}}Z^{\beta}\nabla_{v}^{j}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}}\bigg)

and

\displaystyle E^{T.d.}_{M,B}\geq(\frac{1}{2}-c_{0}\delta_{0}^{-1}C_{m})\lVert\hat{f}\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}}

\displaystyle+2^{-\mathtt{C}_{1}B-2}\sum_{|\beta|\leq B}\sum_{j=0}^{1}\frac{1}{\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{2\beta}}\bigg(\frac{|k|}{\nu}\lVert Z^{\beta}\hat{f}\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}}+(c_{1}-c_{3}\delta_{0}^{-1}\tilde{C}_{m})\lVert Z^{\beta}\nabla_{v}^{j}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}}

(5.49)

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt+(c_{2}-c_{3}\delta_{0}^{-1}\tilde{C}_{m})\lVert\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+jq_{\gamma,s}}Z^{\beta}\nabla_{v}^{j}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}}\bigg).

Having that
M+q_{\gamma,s}>1
, it follows immediately that
E^{T.d}_{M,B}\lesssim E^{e.d.}_{M,B}
(for any
|k|\lesssim\nu
). On the other hand, notice that

(5.50)

\lVert Z^{\beta}\hat{f}\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}}\geq\frac{c_{2}}{4+c_{2}}\lVert Z^{\beta}\boldsymbol{P}\hat{f}\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}}-\frac{c_{2}}{4}\lVert Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}},

where in the last inequality we used
(a+b)^{2}\geq(1-(1+c_{2}/4)^{-1})a^{2}-c_{2}b^{2}/4
. From the integrability properties of the Maxwellian, we also know that

(5.51)

\lVert Z^{\beta}\boldsymbol{P}\hat{f}\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}}\gtrsim\lVert\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M}Z^{\beta}\boldsymbol{P}\hat{f}\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}}+\lVert\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M}\nabla_{v}Z^{\beta}\boldsymbol{P}\hat{f}\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}}.

Hence, combining the two inequalities above, when
|k|\approx\nu
we get

(5.52)

\displaystyle E^{T.d.}_{M,B}\gtrsim\sum_{|\beta|\leq B}\sum_{j=0}^{1}\frac{1}{\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{2\beta}}\bigg(\lVert Z^{\beta}\boldsymbol{P}\hat{f}\rVert^{2}+\tilde{c}\lVert\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+jq_{\gamma,s}}Z^{\beta}\nabla_{v}^{j}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\rVert^{2}\bigg)\gtrsim E^{e.d.}_{M,B},

where in the last inequality we used (
5.46
). For the dissipation energy functionals, the proof is analogous and we omit it.
∎

We are now ready to prove Propositions
5.2
-
5.7
.

5.1.
Linear errors

To prove Proposition
5.2
, we rely on the pointwise-in-
k
estimates (
4.150
) and (
4.163
) that are needed to prove decay for the linearized problem. In fact, the factor
\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle
in the energy functionals replaces the
k
-by-
k
decay in time (which should not be possible in the nonlinear problem) and generates the linear error term (
5.7
). It is therefore natural to expect that the same estimates giving decay in the linearized problem allow us to control this linear error term.

Proof of Proposition
5.2
.

Appealing to (
4.150
) and (
4.163
), recalling that

\varpi=(|\gamma|(2-s)+2s|q_{\gamma,s}|)/(1+s),

for any
R>0
we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}(t)\gtrsim\,

\displaystyle\int_{|k|\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\bigg(\frac{\lambda_{\nu,k}}{R^{\varpi}}\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle^{2J}E^{T.d.}_{M,B}-\frac{\lambda_{\nu,k}}{R^{|\gamma+2s|+2M_{J}}}\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle^{2J}E^{T.d.}_{M+M_{J},B}\bigg){\rm d}k

(5.53)

\displaystyle+\int_{|k|>\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\bigg(\frac{\lambda_{\nu,k}}{R^{\varpi}}\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle^{2J}E^{e.d.}_{M,B}-\frac{\lambda_{\nu,k}}{R^{|\gamma+2s|+2M_{J}}}\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle^{2J}E^{e.d.}_{M+M_{J},B}\bigg){\rm d}k.

The idea here is to choose
R
such that we control
L_{J,M,B}
(
5.7
) with the dissipation and the higher order moments. In particular, we need

(5.54)

\frac{\lambda_{\nu,k}}{R^{\varpi}}\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle^{2J}\sim\lambda_{\nu,k}\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle^{2J-1}\qquad\Longrightarrow\qquad R^{\varpi}=\tilde{\delta}\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle

for some
\tilde{\delta}
sufficiently small to be specified later. Plugging (
5.54
) in (
5.53
), we know that there is a constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}L_{J,M,B}{\rm d}k\leq\tilde{\delta}C\mathcal{D}

(5.55)
