\displaystyle\quad+C\tilde{\delta}^{1-\widetilde{M}_{J}}\int_{|k|\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\lambda_{\nu,k}\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle^{2J-\widetilde{M}_{J}}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{|k|\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}E^{T.d.}_{M+M_{J},B}+\mathbbm{1}_{|k|>\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}E^{e.d.}_{M+M_{J},B}\right){\rm d}k,

where
\widetilde{M}_{J}=(|\gamma+2s|+2M_{J})/\varpi>2J+2
thanks to our choice in (
2.23
).
Then observe that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\lambda_{\nu,k}\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}\tau\right\rangle^{2J-\widetilde{M}_{J}}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{|k|\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}E^{T.d.}_{M+M_{J},B}+\mathbbm{1}_{|k|>\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}E^{e.d.}_{M+M_{J},B}\right)(\tau){\rm d}\tau{\rm d}k

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{0}^{\lambda_{\nu,k}t}\frac{1}{\left\langle s\right\rangle^{\widetilde{M}_{J}-2J}}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{|k|\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}E^{T.d.}_{M+M_{J},B}+\mathbbm{1}_{|k|>\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}E^{e.d.}_{M+M_{J},B}\right)(\lambda_{\nu,k}^{-1}s){\rm d}s{\rm d}k

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\sup_{0\leq s^{\prime}\leq t}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{|k|\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}E^{T.d.}_{M+M_{J},B}+\mathbbm{1}_{|k|>\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}E^{e.d.}_{M+M_{J},B}\right)(s^{\prime}){\rm d}k\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\left\langle s\right\rangle^{2}}{\rm d}s

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{0\leq s^{\prime}\leq t}\mathcal{E}_{mom}(s^{\prime}).

Hence, integrating in time (
5.55
), using the bound above and choosing
\tilde{\delta}
sufficiently small we prove (
5.21
). The proof of (
5.22
) is analogous.
∎

5.2.
Nonlinear errors for the main
L^{2}_{k}
energy

We now turn our attention the proof of Proposition
5.3
. A first crucial ingredient is the trilinear estimate in Lemma
3.6
. This allow us to control everything with
L^{2}_{v}
and
\mathcal{A}
norms, which are used in the definition of the energy and dissipation functionals. Having the trilinear estimate (
3.48
) at hand, the main strategy of proof is to split the nonlinearity to study separately the interactions between high and low
k
-frequencies in each nonlinear term. In particular, we must take care also of
very low
frequencies
|k|\ll\nu
since the behavior in the enhanced dissipation regime is different with respect to the Taylor dispersion one. The main technical difficulty is to choose this splitting carefully to reconstruct the energy and dissipation functionals and to take advantage of the time-decay when needed.

Proof of Proposition
5.3
.

First of all, appealing to the paraproduct decomposition (
2.35
)-(
2.39
), we split the nonlinear term as

(5.56)

\Gamma(f,f)=\sum_{N,N^{\prime}\in 2^{\mathbb{Z}}}\bigg(\Gamma(f_{N},f_{<N/8})+\Gamma(f_{<N/8},f_{N})+\sum_{N/8\leq N^{\prime}\leq 8N}\Gamma(f_{N},f_{N^{\prime}})\bigg)

Due to the structure of the nonlinearity, the treatment of the first two terms inside the parenthesis of (
5.56
) is identical and therefore we will not discuss the term
\Gamma(f_{<N/8},f_{N})
in the sequel. From (
1.11
), one has

(5.57)

\widehat{\Gamma(g,h)}(k)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathcal{Q}(\sqrt{\mu}\hat{g}(\xi),\sqrt{\mu}\hat{h}(k-\xi)){\rm d}\xi.

The nonlinear error terms
NL^{e.d.}_{M,B}
(
5.8
) and
NL^{T.d.}_{M,B}
(
5.12
) have fundamentally different frequency interactions. We thus divide the proof by controlling first the error terms in
NL^{e.d.}_{M,B}
and then the ones in
NL^{T.d.}_{M,B}
.

5.2.1.
Nonlinear enhanced dissipation errors

In this case
|k|\geq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu
. Consider the term
\Gamma(f_{N},f_{<N/8})
. Using the notation in (
5.57
), we have

(5.58)

|k|\leq|k-\xi|+|\xi|\lesssim N,\qquad|k|\geq|\xi|-|k-\xi|\geq N/4\quad\Longrightarrow\quad N\approx|k|\gtrsim\nu.

For
\Gamma(f_{N},f_{N^{\prime}})
, we cannot have
|\xi|\leq(\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu)/64
. Indeed, if the latter inequality were true we would get
|k-\xi|\geq(63\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu)/64
. But this is not possible since otherwise

(5.59)

N^{\prime}\geq|k-\xi|/2,\qquad N\geq N^{\prime}/8,\qquad|\xi|\geq N/2\quad\Longrightarrow\quad|\xi|\geq(\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu)(63/2^{11})

which is a contradiction because
63/2^{11}>1/64
.
Namely, for
\Gamma[f_{N},f_{N^{\prime}}]
, we only have to study the case

N,N^{\prime}\gtrsim\nu.

Thanks to (
5.37
)-(
5.38
), we can always consider
f_{N},f_{N^{\prime}}
to be in the enhanced dissipation regime. On the other hand, the factor
f_{<N/8}
can be in either regimes. For this reason, in the following we only detail how to control the terms arising from
\Gamma(f_{N},f_{<N/8})
. The bounds for the terms involving
\Gamma(f_{N},f_{N^{\prime}})
are easily recovered from the ones for
\Gamma(f_{N},f_{<N/8})
when
f_{<N/8}
is at frequencies
\gtrsim\nu
.

To prove (
5.23
), we first bound the following term in
NL^{e.d.}_{M,B}

(5.60)

\mathcal{I}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M}:=\mathbbm{1}_{|k|\geq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\frac{2^{-\mathtt{C}\beta}}{\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{2\beta}}\left\langle k\right\rangle^{2\alpha}a_{\nu,k}\sum_{N\sim|k|}\left|\left\langle\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}\nabla_{v}Z^{\beta}\mathcal{F}(\Gamma(f_{N},f_{<N/8})),\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}\nabla_{v}Z^{\beta}\hat{f}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\right|.

Then, we split the low-frequency part as

(5.61)

f_{<N/8}=P_{\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}f_{<N/8}+P_{>\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}f_{<N/8},

and define

(5.62)

\mathcal{I}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M}=\mathcal{I}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M,\gtrsim\nu}+\mathcal{I}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M,\lesssim\nu},

where
\mathcal{I}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M,\lesssim\nu}
and
\mathcal{I}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M,\gtrsim\nu}
are the right-hand side of (
5.60
) with
f_{<N/8}
replaced by
P_{\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}f_{<N/8}
and
P_{>\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}f_{<N/8}
respectively.

High-moderately low frequency interactions.

We proceed with the bound for
\mathcal{I}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M,\gtrsim\nu}
.
From the trilinear estimate in Lemma
3.6
and the Leibniz rule for
\Gamma
, we infer

(5.63)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M,\gtrsim\nu}\lesssim\,\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}N\sim|k|\\
|\beta_{1}|+|\beta_{2}|\leq|\beta|\\
j_{1}+j_{2}\leq 1\end{subarray}}\mathbbm{1}_{|k|\geq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}a_{\nu,k}\frac{2^{-\mathtt{C}\beta}}{\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{2\beta}}\left\langle k\right\rangle^{2\alpha}\sqrt{\mathcal{A}[\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}\nabla_{v}Z^{\beta}\hat{f}(k)]}

(5.64)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\bigg(\sqrt{\mathcal{A}[\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}(\nabla_{v})^{j_{1}}Z^{\beta_{1}}\hat{f}_{N}(\xi)]}\left\lVert\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}(\nabla_{v})^{j_{2}}Z^{\beta_{2}}P_{>\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\hat{f}_{<N/8}(k-\xi)\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{v}}

(5.65)

\displaystyle\quad+\left\lVert\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}(\nabla_{v})^{j_{1}}Z^{\beta_{1}}\hat{f}_{N}(\xi)\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{v}}\sqrt{\mathcal{A}[\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}(\nabla_{v})^{j_{2}}Z^{\beta_{2}}P_{>\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\hat{f}_{<N/8}(k-\xi)]}\bigg){\rm d}\xi.

Thanks to (
5.37
), we can always control the terms containing
P_{>\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\hat{f}_{<N/8}
with the functionals in the enhanced dissipation regime. In particular, for the terms involving
\mathcal{A}
we can use the dissipation functional
\mathcal{D}^{e.d.}_{M,B}
(
2.5
) and for the
L^{2}_{v}
terms the energy
E^{e.d.}_{M,B}
(
2.1
). However, when we take
v
-derivatives we need to compare the coefficients
a_{\nu,k}
at different frequencies as in (
5.34
).
To proceed with the bounds, in the following we denote

(5.66)

g^{(\beta,M,j)}=\frac{2^{-\mathtt{C}\beta/2}}{\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{\beta}}\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}(\nabla_{v})^{j}Z^{\beta}\hat{f}.

Since
|\beta_{1}|+|\beta_{2}|\leq|\beta|
and
|\xi|\approx N\approx|k|
, see (
5.58
), we have

(5.67)

\frac{2^{-\mathtt{C}\beta}}{\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{2\beta}}\leq\frac{2^{-\mathtt{C}\beta/2}}{\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{\beta}}\frac{2^{-\mathtt{C}(\beta_{1}+\beta_{2})/2}}{\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{(\beta_{1}+\beta_{2})}},\qquad\left\langle k\right\rangle\approx\left\langle\xi\right\rangle.

Hence, with the notation (
5.66
), using (
5.34
) and the above inequalities in (
5.63
), we get

(5.68)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M,\gtrsim\nu}\lesssim\frac{1}{\nu}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}N\sim|k|\\
|\beta_{1}|+|\beta_{2}|\leq|\beta|\\
j_{1}+j_{2}\leq 1\end{subarray}}\mathbbm{1}_{|k|\geq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\left\langle k\right\rangle^{\alpha}\sqrt{\nu a_{\nu,k}\mathcal{A}[g^{(\beta,M,1)}(k)]}

(5.69)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\bigg(\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{\alpha}\sqrt{\nu(a_{\nu,\xi})^{j_{1}}\mathcal{A}[g^{(\beta_{1},M,j_{1})}_{N}(\xi)]}\sqrt{(a_{\nu,k-\xi})^{j_{2}}}\lVert P_{>\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}g^{(\beta_{2},M,j_{2})}_{<N/8}(k-\xi)\rVert_{L^{2}_{v}}

(5.70)

\displaystyle+\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{\alpha}(\sqrt{a_{\nu,\xi}})^{j_{1}}\lVert g^{(\beta_{1},M,j_{1})}_{N}(\xi)\rVert_{L^{2}_{v}}\sqrt{\nu(a_{\nu,k-\xi})^{j_{2}}\mathcal{A}[P_{>\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}g^{(\beta_{2},M,j_{2})}_{<N/8}(k-\xi)]}\bigg){\rm d}\xi.

We now claim that

(5.71)

\nu\int_{|k|>\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\sum_{\alpha+|\beta|\leq B}\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle^{2J}\mathcal{I}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M,\gtrsim\nu}{\rm d}k\lesssim\sqrt{\mathcal{E}}\mathcal{D}

To prove this bound, we are going to use Cauchy-Schwartz and Young’s convolution inequalities. However, we need to be careful with the number of
x
and
Z
derivatives we have in each term, since the sum of the two must always be less than
B
. Therefore we distinguish two cases.

\diamond

Case

|\beta_{2}|+d/2<B.
Recall the definitions of
E^{e.d.}_{M.B}
and
\mathcal{D}^{e.d.}_{M.B}
given in (
2.1
) and (
2.10
) respectively. Notice that, since
\alpha+|\beta_{1}|\leq B
,
j_{1}\leq 1
and
N\gtrsim\nu
, using also (
5.37
)-(
5.38
), we have

(5.72)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\left\langle k\right\rangle^{\alpha}\sqrt{\nu(a_{\nu,k})^{j_{1}}\mathcal{A}[g^{(\beta_{1},M,j_{1})}_{N}]}\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{k}}\lesssim\left\lVert\sqrt{\mathcal{D}^{e.d.}_{M,B}(k)_{N}}\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{k}}

(5.73)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\left\langle k\right\rangle^{\alpha}(\sqrt{a_{\nu,k}})^{j_{1}}g^{(\beta_{1},M,j_{1})}_{N}\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{k}}\lesssim\left\lVert\sqrt{E^{e.d.}_{M,B}(k)_{N}}\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{k}}

Hence, from the quasi-orthogonality of the Littlewood-Paley projection, the Cauchy-Schwartz and the Young’s convolution inequalities, combining (
5.68
) with (
5.72
)-
(
5.73
) we get

(5.74)

\displaystyle\nu

\displaystyle\int_{|k|>\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha+|\beta|\leq B\\
|\beta_{2}|+(d+1)/2<B\end{subarray}}\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle^{2J}\mathcal{I}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M,\gtrsim\nu}{\rm d}k\lesssim\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}N\gtrsim\nu\\
|\beta_{2}|+(d+1)/2<B,\,j_{2}\leq 1\end{subarray}}\left\lVert\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle^{J}\sqrt{\mathcal{D}^{e.d.}_{M,B}(k)_{\sim N}}\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{k}}

(5.75)

\displaystyle\times\bigg(\left\lVert\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle^{J}\sqrt{\mathcal{D}^{e.d.}_{M,B}(k)_{N}}\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{k}}\left\lVert\sqrt{(a_{\nu,k})^{j_{2}}}P_{>\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}g^{(\beta_{2},M,j_{2})}_{<N/8}\right\rVert_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{v}}

(5.76)

\displaystyle\quad+\left\lVert\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle^{J}\sqrt{E^{e.d.}_{M,B}(k)_{N}}\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{k}}\left\lVert\sqrt{\nu(a_{\nu,k})^{j_{2}}\mathcal{A}[P_{>\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}g^{(\beta_{2},M,j_{2})}_{<N/8}(k)]}\right\rVert_{L^{1}_{k}}\bigg).

From Hölder’s inequality we deduce

(5.77)

\left\lVert\sqrt{(a_{\nu,k})^{j_{2}}}P_{>\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}g^{(\beta_{2},M,j_{2})}_{<N/8}\right\rVert_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{v}}\lesssim_{\delta_{*}}\left\lVert\left\langle k\right\rangle^{d/2+\delta_{*}}\sqrt{a_{\nu,k}}^{j_{2}}P_{>\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}g^{(\beta_{2},M,j_{2})}_{<N/8}\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{k}L^{2}_{v}}

where
\delta_{*}>0
is an arbitrary small number. In the case under consideration, it is enough to choose
\delta_{*}
such that
|\beta_{2}|+d/2+\delta_{*}\leq B
to obtain

(5.78)

\left\lVert\left\langle k\right\rangle^{d/2+\delta_{*}}\sqrt{(a_{\nu,k})^{j_{2}}}P_{>\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}g^{(\beta_{2},M,j_{2})}_{<N/8}\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{k}L^{2}_{v}}\lesssim\left\lVert\sqrt{P_{>\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}E^{e.d.}_{M,B}(k)_{<N/8}}\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{k}}.

Analogously, we get

(5.79)

\left\lVert\sqrt{\nu(a_{\nu,k})^{j_{2}}\mathcal{A}[P_{>\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}g^{(\beta_{2},M,j_{2})}_{<N/8}]}\right\rVert_{L^{1}_{k}}\lesssim\left\lVert\sqrt{P_{>\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\mathcal{D}^{e.d.}_{M,B}(k)_{<N/8}}\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{k}}

Therefore, combining (
5.75
) with (
5.78
) and (
5.79
), using the quasi orthogonality of the Littlewood-Paley projections, we infer

(5.80)

\displaystyle\nu\int_{|k|\geq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha+|\beta|\leq B\\
|\beta_{2}|+(d+1)/2<B\end{subarray}}\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle^{2J}\mathcal{I}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M,\gtrsim\nu}{\rm d}k\lesssim\sqrt{\mathcal{E}}\mathcal{D}.

\diamond

Case

|\beta_{2}|+d/2\geq B:
In this case we cannot pay
x
-derivatives on the low frequency factors.
Then, since
\alpha+|\beta|\leq B
and
|\beta_{1}|+|\beta_{2}|\leq|\beta|
, observe that

(5.81)

\alpha+d/2+|\beta_{1}|\leq B+d/2-|\beta_{2}|\leq d<B.

Hence, when applying the Young’s convolution inequality to (
5.68
) we can use the
L^{1}_{k}
norm in the factors at frequencies
N
in (
5.69
)-(
5.70
). Namely, using (
5.72
)-(
5.73
) with

(\alpha,j_{1},\beta_{1},N)\to(0,j_{2},\beta_{2},<N/8),

similarly to (
5.75
), one has

(5.82)

\displaystyle\nu

\displaystyle\int_{|k|>\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha+|\beta|\leq B\\
|\beta_{2}|+(d+1)/2\geq B\end{subarray}}\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle^{2J}\mathcal{I}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M,\gtrsim\nu}{\rm d}k\lesssim\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}N\gtrsim\nu,\,|\beta_{1}|+|\beta_{2}|\leq B\\
|\beta_{2}|+(d+1)/2\geq B,\,j_{1}\leq 1\end{subarray}}\left\lVert\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle^{J}\sqrt{\mathcal{D}^{e.d.}_{M,B}(k)_{\sim N}}\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{k}}

(5.83)

\displaystyle\times\bigg(\left\lVert\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle^{J}\left\langle k\right\rangle^{\alpha}\sqrt{\nu(a_{\nu,k})^{j_{1}}\mathcal{A}[g^{(\beta_{1},M,j_{1})}_{N}(k)]}\right\rVert_{L^{1}_{k}}\left\lVert\sqrt{E^{e.d.}_{M,B}(k)_{<N/8}}\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{k}}

(5.84)

\displaystyle\quad+\left\lVert\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle^{J}\left\langle k\right\rangle^{\alpha}\sqrt{(a_{\nu,k})^{j_{1}}}g^{(\beta_{1},M,j_{1})}_{N}\right\rVert_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{v}}\left\lVert\sqrt{\mathcal{D}^{e.d.}_{M,B}(k)_{<N/8}}\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{k}}\bigg).

Thus, thanks to (
5.81
), we can argue as done in (
5.77
)-(
5.79
) for the factors at frequencies
N
. So we conclude that

(5.85)

\displaystyle\nu\int_{|k|\geq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha+|\beta|\leq B\\
|\beta_{2}|+(d+1)/2\geq B\end{subarray}}\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle^{2J}\mathcal{I}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M,\gtrsim\nu}{\rm d}k\lesssim\sqrt{\mathcal{E}}\mathcal{D},

which proves the claim (
5.71
) by combining (
5.80
) and (
5.85
).

Remark 5.9
.

Observe that to get (
5.71
) we never used the time-decay to control the nonlinearity. The main reason for this is that all factors are in the enhanced dissipation regime. Therefore, if instead of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
we consider
\mathbb{T}^{d}
, the nonlinear error term under consideration is bounded by (
5.71
). Since the other error terms enjoy analogous estimates, the proof for the bound (
5.23
) would be almost over (one has to control the interactions between the
k=0
mode and
k\neq 0
separately). On the other hand, to control our energy functionals in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we cannot use the available dissipation for
k
too small and the time-decay will play a crucial role.
3
3

3

A similar issue was already present for the energy functional used by Strain
[
61
]
. However, due to the structure of the energy functional in
[
61
]
, it is not necessary to exploit the decay in time and estimates of the form
\sqrt{\mathcal{E}}\mathcal{D}
are enough to close the argument. On the other hand, exploiting the time-decay in the nonlinear problem seems crucial to prove the Taylor dispersion, the enhanced dissipation and the optimal time-decay rates obtained by controlling the
L^{\infty}_{k}
norm of the solution.

High-very low frequency interactions.

We now turn our attention to
\mathcal{I}^{\alpha,\beta}_{a,\lesssim\nu}
, which we recall is defined as the right-hand side of (
5.60
) with
f_{<N/8}
replaced by
P_{\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}f_{<N/8}
. In this case the low-frequencies
<N/8
are at very low frequencies, namely in the Taylor dispersion regime, in which we do not have
k
-dependent coefficients in front of
v
-derivatives in
E^{T.d.}_{M,B}
(
2.10
). Therefore, exploiting that
a_{\nu,k}\lesssim 1
for
|k|\gtrsim\nu
, we bound
\mathcal{I}^{\alpha,\beta}_{a,\lesssim\nu}
as the right-hand side of (
5.68
) with
P_{>\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\to P_{\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}
and
\left\langle k-\xi\right\rangle\sqrt{a_{\nu,k-\xi}}\to 1
. More precisely, recalling the notation for
g^{*}
introduced in (
5.66
), we have

(5.86)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M,\lesssim\nu}\lesssim\,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\nu}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}N\sim|k|\\
|\beta_{1}|+|\beta_{2}|\leq|\beta|\\
j_{1}+j_{2}\leq 1\end{subarray}}\mathbbm{1}_{|k|\geq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\left\langle k\right\rangle^{\alpha}\sqrt{\nu a_{\nu,k}\mathcal{A}[g^{(\beta,M,1)}(k)]}

(5.87)

\displaystyle\times\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\bigg(\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{\alpha}\sqrt{\nu(a_{\nu,\xi})^{j_{1}}\mathcal{A}[g^{(\beta_{1},M,j_{1})}_{N}(\xi)]}\lVert P_{\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}g^{(\beta_{2},M,j_{2})}_{<N/8}(k-\xi)\rVert_{L^{2}_{v}}

(5.88)

\displaystyle\qquad+\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{\alpha}\sqrt{(a_{\nu,\xi})^{j_{1}}}\lVert g^{(\beta_{1},M,j_{1})}_{N}(\xi)\rVert_{L^{2}_{v}}\sqrt{\nu\mathcal{A}[P_{\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}g^{(\beta_{2},M,j_{2})}_{<N/8}(k-\xi)]}\bigg){\rm d}\xi.

We claim that

(5.89)

\nu\int_{|k|>\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\sum_{\alpha+|\beta|\leq B}\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle^{2J}\mathcal{I}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M,\lesssim\nu}{\rm d}k\lesssim\sqrt{\mathcal{E}}\sqrt{\mathcal{D}}\left(\sqrt{\mathcal{D}}+\big(\nu^{d}\mathbbm{1}_{t\leq\nu^{-1}}+\left\langle\nu/t\right\rangle^{\frac{d}{2}}\mathbbm{1}_{t>\nu^{-1}}\big)\sqrt{\mathcal{E}_{LF}}\right).

Observe that the first piece on the right-hand side of (
5.89
) is exactly as in (
5.71
). On the other hand, we will see that to handle the interactions between two very different frequency regimes we need to exploit all the available information about the solution.

To prove (
5.89
), we need to use the micro-macro decomposition, which is a key ingredient at very low frequencies. In light of (
5.66
), it is convenient to denote
4
4

4

This extra notation is introduced because
[Z,\boldsymbol{P}]\neq 0
.

(5.90)

g^{(\beta,M,j)}_{micro}=\frac{2^{-\mathtt{C}\beta/2}}{\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{\beta}}\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}(\nabla_{v})^{j}Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}.

To control the term involving only the
L^{2}_{v}
-norm at very low-frequencies in (
5.87
), we exploit the properties of the Maxwellian to get

(5.91)

\displaystyle\lVert P_{\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}g^{(\beta_{2},M,j_{2})}_{<N/8}(k-\xi)\rVert_{L^{2}_{v}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{0\leq|\tilde{\beta}|\leq|\beta_{2}|}\frac{2^{-\mathtt{C}\tilde{\beta}/2}}{\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{\tilde{\beta}}}P_{\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}(|t(k-\xi)|^{\tilde{\beta}}|(\hat{\rho},\hat{\mathsf{m}},\hat{\mathsf{e}})(k-\xi)_{<N/8}|)

\displaystyle+\left\lVert P_{\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}g^{(\beta_{2},M,j_{2})}_{micro}(k-\xi)_{<N/8}\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{v}}.

Since
E^{T.d}_{M,B}
(
2.10
),
\mathcal{D}^{T.d}_{M,B}
(
2.16
) and
E^{e.d.}_{M,B}
(
2.1
),
\mathcal{D}^{e.d.}_{M,B}
(
2.5
) control the same quantities for the microscopic part (as observed after the definition of
E^{T.d}_{M,B}
), we can proceed as done to obtain (
5.71
) for all the terms with
g^{*}_{micro}
. We therefore do not detail such bounds. On the other hand, when macroscopic quantities are involved we must proceed in a different way.

First of all, when
|\tilde{\beta}|=0
we can readily reconstruct the energy functional in view of the term
\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{v}}
in
E^{T.d}_{M,B}
.
For
|\tilde{\beta}|\geq 1
, since
|k-\xi|\lesssim\nu
, one has

(5.92)

|t(k-\xi)|^{\tilde{\beta}}\lesssim\nu t\sqrt{\frac{|k-\xi|}{\nu}}|t(k-\xi)|^{\tilde{\beta}-1}.

Thus, in view of Lemma
4.3
, we deduce that

(5.93)

\displaystyle\frac{2^{-\mathtt{C}\tilde{\beta}/2}}{\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{\tilde{\beta}}}P_{\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}(|t(k-\xi)|^{\tilde{\beta}}|(\hat{\rho},\hat{\mathsf{m}},\hat{\mathsf{e}})(k-\xi)_{<N/8}|)\lesssim\lVert P_{\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\hat{f}_{<N/8}(k-\xi)\rVert_{L^{2}_{v}}

(5.94)

\displaystyle\qquad+\nu t\frac{2^{-\mathtt{C}\tilde{\beta}/2}}{\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{\tilde{\beta}}}\sum_{1\leq|\tilde{\beta}^{\prime}|\leq|\tilde{\beta}|-1}P_{\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{|k-\xi|}{\nu}}\lVert Z^{\tilde{\beta}^{\prime}}\hat{f}_{<N/8}(k-\xi)\rVert_{L^{2}_{v}}\bigg).

For the first term on the right-hand side of (
5.93
) we can again exploit the term
\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{v}}
in
E^{T.d}_{M,B}
. For the remaining ones,
observe that, since
|\tilde{\beta}^{\prime}|\leq|\tilde{\beta}|-1
we have

(5.95)

\nu t\frac{2^{-\mathtt{C}\tilde{\beta}/2}}{\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{\tilde{\beta}}}\leq\frac{2^{-\mathtt{C}\tilde{\beta}^{\prime}/2}}{\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{\tilde{\beta}^{\prime}}},

so we are able to absorb the factor
\nu t

in (
5.94
). Recalling that in the definition of
E^{T.d.}_{M,b}
(
2.10
) we have the term
\sqrt{\frac{|k|}{\nu}}\left\lVert Z^{\beta}f\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{v}}
, we finally obtain

(5.96)

\sum_{0\leq|\tilde{\beta}|\leq|\beta_{2}|}\frac{2^{-\mathtt{C}\tilde{\beta}/2}}{\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{\tilde{\beta}}}P_{\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}(|t(k-\xi)|^{\tilde{\beta}}|(\hat{\rho},\hat{\mathsf{m}},\hat{\mathsf{e}})(k-\xi)_{<N/8}|)\lesssim\sqrt{P_{\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}E^{T.d.}_{M,B}(k-\xi)_{<N/8}}.

Notice that here we do not need to pay attention to the number of
x
-derivatives since these terms are compactly supported in the Fourier space. Therefore, the terms arising from (
5.87
) are controlled by
\sqrt{\mathcal{E}}\mathcal{D}
when proving (
5.89
).

Consider now the terms in (
5.88
), where very low frequencies are computed in the
\mathcal{A}
-norm. By the micro-macro decomposition and standard properties of the Maxwellian, we get

(5.97)

\displaystyle\sqrt{\nu\mathcal{A}[P_{\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}g^{(\beta_{2},M,j_{2})}_{<N/8}(k-\xi)]}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\nu}\sum_{0\leq|\tilde{\beta}|\leq|\beta_{2}|}\frac{2^{-\mathtt{C}\tilde{\beta}/2}}{\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{\tilde{\beta}}}P_{\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}(|t(k-\xi)|^{\tilde{\beta}}|(\hat{\rho},\hat{\mathsf{m}},\hat{\mathsf{e}})(k-\xi)_{<N/8}|)

(5.98)

\displaystyle\quad+\sqrt{\nu\mathcal{A}\left[P_{\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}g^{(\beta_{2},M,j_{2})}_{micro}(k-\xi)_{<N/8}\right]}.

As observed previously, since
E^{e.d.}_{M,B}
and
E^{T.d.}_{M,B}
have the same structure for microscopic quantities, we can proceed as done to obtain (
5.71
) to control the term in (
5.98
) (with the simplification that one can always pay derivatives at very low frequencies). On the other hand, for the macroscopic part we need to be more careful due to the degeneracy of the dissipation for very low
k
’s. In fact, we first need to improve the bound (
5.94
) to

(5.99)

\displaystyle\frac{2^{-\mathtt{C}\tilde{\beta}/2}}{\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{\tilde{\beta}}}P_{\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}(|t(k-\xi)|^{\tilde{\beta}}|(\hat{\rho},\hat{\mathsf{m}},\hat{\mathsf{e}})(k-\xi)_{<N/8}|)\lesssim\lVert P_{\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\hat{f}_{<N/8}(k-\xi)\rVert_{L^{2}_{v}}

(5.100)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\nu}}\sum_{1\leq|\tilde{\beta}^{\prime}|\leq|\tilde{\beta}|-1}\frac{2^{-\mathtt{C}\tilde{\beta}^{\prime}/2}}{\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{\tilde{\beta}^{\prime}}}\frac{|k-\xi|}{\sqrt{\nu}}\lVert P_{\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}Z^{\tilde{\beta}^{\prime}}\hat{f}_{<N/8}(k-\xi)\rVert_{L^{2}_{v}},

where we used

(5.101)

|t(k-\xi)|^{\tilde{\beta}^{\prime}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\nu}}(\nu t)\frac{|k-\xi|}{\sqrt{\nu}}|t(k-\xi)|^{\tilde{\beta}^{\prime}-1}

and the fact that
1\leq|\tilde{\beta}^{\prime}|\leq|\tilde{\beta}|-1
to absorb the
\nu t
loss.
Notice that we can control a term like
\frac{|k|}{\sqrt{\nu}}\left\lVert Z^{\beta}f\right\rVert
with
\mathcal{D}^{T.d}_{M,B}
(
2.16
). In particular, from (
5.99
) we infer

\displaystyle\sqrt{\nu}\sum_{0\leq|\tilde{\beta}|\leq|\beta_{2}|}

\displaystyle\frac{2^{-\mathtt{C}\tilde{\beta}/2}}{\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{\tilde{\beta}}}P_{\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}(|t(k-\xi)|^{\tilde{\beta}}|(\hat{\rho},\hat{\mathsf{m}},\hat{\mathsf{e}})(k-\xi)_{<N/8}|)

(5.102)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\nu}\lVert P_{\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\hat{f}_{<N/8}(k-\xi)\rVert_{L^{2}_{v}}+\sqrt{P_{\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\mathcal{D}^{T.d.}_{M,B}(k-\xi)_{<N/8}}.

Then, the last term on the right-hand side of (
5.102
) gives rise to terms that are bounded by
\sqrt{\mathcal{E}}\mathcal{D}
. However, for the other term we cannot exploit the dissipation functional since we would need a factor
|k-\xi|
that can be extremely small in this regime. Thus, with the strategy outlined in (
5.91
)-(
5.102
) we are able to prove

(5.103)

\displaystyle\nu\int_{|k|<\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle^{2J}\mathcal{I}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M,\lesssim\nu}{\rm d}k

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\mathcal{E}}\mathcal{D}+\sqrt{\mathcal{E}}\sqrt{\mathcal{D}}\lVert P_{\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\hat{f}\rVert_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{v}}.

Then, since
\lambda_{\nu,k}\sim|k|^{2}/\nu
for
|k|\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu
, notice that

(5.104)

\displaystyle\lVert P_{\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\hat{f}\rVert_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{v}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lVert P_{\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle^{J^{\prime}}\hat{f}\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{k}L^{2}_{v}}\int_{|k|\lesssim\nu}\frac{{\rm d}\xi}{\left\langle\nu^{-1}|\xi|^{2}t\right\rangle^{J^{\prime}}}

(5.105)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\nu^{d}\mathbbm{1}_{t\leq\nu^{-1}}+\left\langle\nu/t\right\rangle^{\frac{d}{2}}\mathbbm{1}_{t>\nu^{-1}}\right)\sqrt{\mathcal{E}_{LF}}.

Inserting this bound in (
5.103
) we finally prove (
5.89
). Combining (
5.71
) and (
5.89
) we finish the proof of the bound (
5.23
) for the term
\mathcal{I}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M}
(
5.60
) in
NL^{e.d.}_{M,B}
(
5.8
).

Remark 5.10
.

For
d\geq 3
it is not necessary to control the energy functional
\mathcal{E}_{LF}
in order to bound the nonlinear terms coming from the
L^{2}_{k}
energy functional, i.e.
\mathcal{E}
. Indeed, we can use the
L^{2}_{k}
norm instead of the
L^{\infty}_{k}
norm in (
5.104
) to get

(5.106)

\displaystyle\lVert P_{\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\hat{f}\rVert_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{v}}\lesssim\left(\nu^{\frac{d}{2}}\mathbbm{1}_{t\leq\nu^{-1}}+\left\langle\nu/t\right\rangle^{\frac{d}{4}}\mathbbm{1}_{t>\nu^{-1}}\right)\sqrt{\mathcal{E}}.

However, for
d=2
this term would generate a logarithmic loss when doing estimates as in (
5.31
). Besides getting sharp decay estimate, this is the other main reason why we directly control
\mathcal{E}
in terms of the
\mathcal{E}_{LF}
even for
d\geq 3
.

Regarding the other terms in
NL^{e.d.}_{M,B}
, notice that the first term on the right-hand side of (
5.8
) is easier to control with respect to (
5.60
) since no
v
or
x
derivatives are involved.
To handle the terms with
b_{\nu,k}
in (
5.8
), recalling the definition of
b_{\nu,k}
(
2.4
), it is not hard to check that the factor
|k|^{-1}
balances the
x
-derivative whereas the remaining
(\nu/|k|)^{\frac{1}{1+2s}}
is exactly
\sqrt{a_{\nu,k}}
, which is the coefficient we need to reconstruct the energy and dissipation in the terms involving
v
-derivatives. Indeed, for these terms at most one factor has a
v
-derivative as opposed to
\mathcal{I}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M}
where two factors can have a
v
-derivative. Therefore, taking into account of the scalings of the coefficients, the estimates for these terms are also analogous to one for
\mathcal{I}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M}
. This finishes the proof of the bounds (
5.23
) and (
5.24
) for the nonlinearities coming from the enhanced dissipation energy functional.

5.2.2.
Nonlinear Taylor dispersion errors

We now consider the nonlinear error terms related to the Taylor dispersion energy functional, see (
5.12
). In this regime we have
|k|\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu
. We use again the paraproduct decomposition (
5.56
). When studying the terms containing
\Gamma(f_{N},f_{<N/8})
, thanks to (
5.58
) we know that both
f_{N}
and
f_{<N/8}
are in the Taylor dispersion regime (namely
|\xi|,|k-\xi|\lesssim\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu
). Instead, for
\Gamma(f_{N},f_{N^{\prime}})
we can have

(5.107)

|\xi|\leq 2\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu\quad\Longrightarrow\quad|k-\xi|\leq 3\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu\qquad\text{or}\qquad|\xi|>2\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu\quad\Longrightarrow\quad|k-\xi|>3\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu.

This means that
f_{N}
and
f_{N^{\prime}}
are either in the Taylor dispersion regime or in the enhanced dissipation one, namely

(5.108)

N^{\prime}\sim N\gtrsim\nu,\qquad\text{or}\qquad N^{\prime}\sim N\lesssim\nu.

Consequently, we will only detail the treatment of the error terms arising from
\Gamma(f_{N},f_{N^{\prime}})
since the bounds when
N\lesssim\nu
easily adapt to
\Gamma(f_{N},f_{<N/8})
.

Let us consider first the term from (
5.14
), that is the analogue of (
5.60
), namely

(5.109)

\mathcal{J}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M}:=\sum_{N\sim N^{\prime}}\mathbbm{1}_{|k|\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\frac{2^{-\mathtt{C}_{1}\beta}}{\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{2\beta}}\left|\left\langle\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}\nabla_{v}Z^{\beta}\mathcal{F}(\Gamma(f_{N},f_{N^{\prime}})),\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}\nabla_{v}Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\right|.

We define

(5.110)

\mathcal{J}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M}=\mathcal{J}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M,\lesssim\nu}+\mathcal{J}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M,\gtrsim\nu},

where

\mathcal{J}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M,\lesssim\nu}
and
\mathcal{J}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M,\gtrsim\nu}
are (
5.109
) with
N\sim N^{\prime}\lesssim\nu
and
N\sim N^{\prime}\gtrsim\nu
respectively.

Very low-very low frequency interactions
. When
N\lesssim\nu
, thanks to (
5.37
) we can consider
f_{N},f_{N^{\prime}}
and
(I-\boldsymbol{P})f
in the Taylor dispersion regime. We can then apply Lemma
3.6
to obtain estimates as in (
5.63
). Having that

\nu\mathcal{A}[\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}\nabla_{v}Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}]

is a term included in
\mathcal{D}^{T.d}_{M,B}
(
2.16
), we can proceed as done to obtain the bound (
5.89
) in the previous subsection to get

(5.111)

\nu\int_{|k|<\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\sum_{\alpha+|\beta|\leq B}\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle^{2J}\mathcal{J}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M,\lesssim\nu}{\rm d}k\lesssim\sqrt{\mathcal{E}}\sqrt{\mathcal{D}}\bigg(\sqrt{\mathcal{D}}+\left(\nu^{d}\mathbbm{1}_{t\leq\nu^{-1}}+\left\langle\nu/t\right\rangle^{\frac{d}{2}}\mathbbm{1}_{t>\nu^{-1}}\right)\sqrt{\mathcal{E}_{LF}}\bigg).

Very low-high frequency interactions
. On the other hand, for
N\gtrsim\nu
we have to be more careful since now we need to reconstruct the
a_{\nu,k}
coeffiecients in
E^{e.d.}_{M,B}
and
\mathcal{D}^{e.d.}_{M,B}
which could potentially lead to losses of order
\nu^{-1/(1+2s)}
. This is because
v
-derivatives in the Taylor dispersion regime are not weighted by factors
\nu^{-1/(1+2s)}
as in the enhanced dissipation one. To overcome this difficulty, using the same notation introduced in (
5.66
), appealing to Lemma
3.6
we obtain

(5.112)

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M,\gtrsim\nu}\lesssim\frac{1}{\nu}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}N\sim N^{\prime}\gtrsim\nu\\
|\beta_{1}|+|\beta_{2}|\leq|\beta|,\,j_{1}+j_{2}\leq 1\end{subarray}}\mathbbm{1}_{|k|\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\left\langle k\right\rangle^{\alpha}\frac{2^{-\mathtt{C}_{1}\beta/2}}{\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{\beta}}\sqrt{\nu\mathcal{A}[\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}\nabla_{v}Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}(k)]}

\displaystyle\times\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\bigg(\left(\frac{\left\langle\xi\right\rangle}{\nu}\right)^{\frac{j_{1}}{1+2s}}\sqrt{\nu(a_{\nu,\xi})^{j_{1}}\mathcal{A}[g^{(\beta_{1},M,j_{1})}_{N}(\xi)]}\left(\frac{\left\langle k-\xi\right\rangle}{\nu}\right)^{\frac{j_{2}}{1+2s}}\sqrt{(a_{\nu,k-\xi})^{j_{2}}}\lVert g^{(\beta_{2},M,j_{2})}_{N^{\prime}}(k-\xi)\rVert_{L^{2}_{v}}

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{\left\langle\xi\right\rangle}{\nu}\right)^{\frac{j_{1}}{1+2s}}\sqrt{(a_{\nu,\xi})^{j_{1}}}\lVert g^{(\beta_{1},M,j_{1})}_{N}(\xi)\rVert_{L^{2}_{v}}\left(\frac{\left\langle k-\xi\right\rangle}{\nu}\right)^{\frac{j_{2}}{1+2s}}\sqrt{\nu(a_{\nu,k-\xi})^{j_{2}}\mathcal{A}[g^{(\beta_{2},M,1)}_{N^{\prime}}(k-\xi)]}\bigg){\rm d}\xi.

To absorb the loss in
\nu
when estimating the
L^{2}_{k}
norm, we use the fact that we are integrating on frequencies
|k|\lesssim\nu
to gain powers of
\nu
from the integration. Namely, we eploit the inequality

(5.113)

\displaystyle\int_{|k|\lesssim\nu}|{G}(k)({H}*Q)(k)|{\rm d}k\lesssim\left\lVert H*Q\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{k}}\left\lVert G\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{k}}\nu^{\frac{d}{2}}\lesssim\left\lVert H\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{k}}\left\lVert Q\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{k}}\left\lVert G\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{k}}\nu^{\frac{d}{2}}.

Using that
N\sim N^{\prime}
, we can distribute the
x
-derivative in (
5.112
) on the term with the lowest number of
Z
-derivatives (smaller
|\beta_{i}|
).
Hence, combining the inequality (
5.35
) with (
5.112
) and using (
5.113
), we get

(5.114)

\displaystyle\nu\int_{|k|\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\sum_{\alpha+|\beta|\leq B}\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle^{2J}\mathcal{J}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M,\gtrsim\nu}{\rm d}k\lesssim\nu^{\frac{d}{2}-\frac{1}{1+2s}}\left\lVert P_{\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle^{J}\sqrt{\mathcal{D}^{T.d.}_{M,B}}\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{k}}

(5.115)

\displaystyle\times\bigg(\left\lVert P_{>\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle^{J}\sqrt{\mathcal{D}^{e.d.}_{M,B}}\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{k}}\left\lVert P_{>\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\sqrt{E^{e.d.}_{M,B}}\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{k}}

(5.116)

\displaystyle+\left\lVert P_{>\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle^{J}\sqrt{E^{e.d.}_{M,B}}\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{k}}\left\lVert P_{>\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\sqrt{\mathcal{D}^{e.d.}_{M,B}}\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{k}}\bigg)

(5.117)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\mathcal{E}}\mathcal{D},

where we also used that
d/2-1/(1+2s)\geq 0
since
d\geq 2
and
s\in(0,1]
.
Combining (
5.111
) and (
5.117
) we see that from the term
\mathcal{J}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M}
we get bounds in agreement with (
5.23
). Following the same arguments outlined for the proof of the bounds for
\mathcal{J}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M}
, it is not difficult to control in an analogous way the terms in (
5.12
)-(
5.16
) containing at least one
(I-\boldsymbol{P})f
.

Macroscopic error terms.

We are thus left with the macroscopic error terms given by

(5.118)

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}^{\alpha,\beta}_{macro,N\sim N^{\prime}}+\mathbbm{1}_{|k|\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\frac{c_{0}}{\nu}\mathcal{M}_{\Gamma,N\sim N^{\prime}},

(5.119)

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}^{\alpha,\beta}_{macro,N\sim N^{\prime}}:=\sum_{N\sim N^{\prime}}\mathbbm{1}_{|k|\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\frac{2^{-\mathtt{C}_{0}\beta}}{\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{2\beta}}\left\langle k\right\rangle^{2\alpha}\bigg|\frac{|k|}{\nu}\left\langle Z^{\beta}\mathcal{F}(\Gamma(f_{N},f_{N^{\prime}})),Z^{\beta}\boldsymbol{P}\hat{f}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\bigg|

where
\mathcal{M}_{\Gamma,N\sim N^{\prime}}
is defined as in (
5.17
) with
\Gamma(f,f)
replaced by
\sum_{N\sim N^{\prime}}\Gamma(f_{N},f_{N^{\prime}})
. We recall that the proof of the bounds for the terms with
\Gamma(f_{N},f_{<N/8})
can be treated as a subcase of the one we present below, see also the discussion at beginning of the proof for the Taylor dispersion errors.

We claim that

(5.120)

\displaystyle\nu\int_{|k|\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\sum_{\alpha+|\beta|\leq B}\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle^{2J}\big(\mathcal{J}^{\alpha,\beta}_{macro,N\sim N^{\prime}}+\frac{c_{0}}{\nu}\mathcal{M}_{\Gamma,N\sim N^{\prime}}\big){\rm d}k

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\mathcal{E}}\mathcal{D}.

Indeed, using the trilinear estimate in Lemma
3.6
we get

(5.121)

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}^{\alpha,\beta}_{macro,N\sim N^{\prime}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\nu}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}N\sim N^{\prime}\\
|\beta_{1}|+|\beta_{2}|\leq|\beta|\end{subarray}}\mathbbm{1}_{|k|\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\frac{2^{-\mathtt{C}_{0}\beta/2}}{\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{\beta}}\left\langle k\right\rangle^{\alpha}\frac{|k|}{\sqrt{\nu}}\lVert Z^{\beta}\boldsymbol{P}\hat{f}(k)\rVert_{L^{2}_{v}}

(5.122)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\bigg(\frac{2^{-\mathtt{C}_{0}\beta_{1}/2}}{\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{\beta_{1}}}\sqrt{\nu\mathcal{A}[Z^{\beta_{1}}\hat{f}_{N}(\xi)]}\frac{2^{-\mathtt{C}_{0}\beta_{2}/2}}{\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{\beta_{2}}}\left\lVert Z^{\beta_{2}}\hat{f}_{N}(k-\xi)\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{v}}

(5.123)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{2^{-\mathtt{C}_{0}\beta_{1}/2}}{\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{\beta_{1}}}\left\lVert Z^{\beta_{1}}\hat{f}_{N}(\xi)\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{v}}\frac{2^{-\mathtt{C}_{0}\beta_{2}/2}}{\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{\beta_{2}}}\sqrt{\nu\mathcal{A}[Z^{\beta_{2}}\hat{f}_{N}(k-\xi)]}\bigg){\rm d}\xi

where we used that
\left\langle k\right\rangle^{\alpha}\lesssim 1
for
|k|\lesssim\nu
. From the bound above, exploiting the inequality (
5.113
) we get

(5.124)

\displaystyle\nu\int_{|k|\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\sum_{\alpha+|\beta|\leq B}\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle^{2J}\mathcal{J}^{\alpha,\beta}_{macro,N\sim N^{\prime}}{\rm d}k

\displaystyle\lesssim\nu^{\frac{d}{2}}\left\lVert P_{\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle^{J}\sqrt{\mathcal{D}^{T.d.}_{M,B}}\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{k}}\sqrt{\mathcal{E}}\sqrt{\mathcal{D}}

(5.125)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\mathcal{E}}\mathcal{D}.

Notice that here we never lose factors
\nu^{-1/(1+2s)}
because we do not have
v
-derivatives.
For
\mathcal{M}_{\Gamma,N\sim N^{\prime}}
, in view of Lemma (
4.3
), it is not hard to see that

(5.126)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{\Gamma,N\sim N^{\prime}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\nu}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}N\sim N^{\prime}\\
|\beta_{1}|+|\beta_{2}|\leq|\beta|\end{subarray}}\mathbbm{1}_{|k|\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\sum_{\widetilde{\beta}\leq\beta}\frac{2^{-\mathtt{C}_{1}\widetilde{\beta}/2}}{\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{\widetilde{\beta}}}\left\langle k\right\rangle^{\alpha}\frac{|k|}{\sqrt{\nu}}\lVert Z^{\beta}\boldsymbol{P}\hat{f}(k)\rVert_{L^{2}_{v}}

(5.127)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\bigg(\frac{2^{-\mathtt{C}_{1}\beta_{1}}}{\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{\beta_{1}}}\sqrt{\nu\mathcal{A}[Z^{\beta_{1}}\hat{f}_{N}(\xi)]}\frac{2^{-\mathtt{C}_{1}\beta_{2}}}{\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{\beta_{2}}}\left\lVert Z^{\beta_{2}}\hat{f}_{N}(k-\xi)\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{v}}

(5.128)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{2^{-\mathtt{C}_{1}\beta_{1}}}{\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{\beta_{1}}}\left\lVert Z^{\beta_{1}}\hat{f}_{N}(\xi)\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{v}}\frac{2^{-\mathtt{C}_{1}\beta_{2}}}{\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{\beta_{2}}}\sqrt{\nu\mathcal{A}[Z^{\beta_{2}}\hat{f}_{N}(k-\xi)]}\bigg){\rm d}\xi.

Therefore, also for this term we deduce a bound in agreement with (
5.120
), whence concluding the proof of Proposition
5.3
.
∎

5.3.
Nonlinear errors for the main
L^{\infty}_{k}
energy

In this section we aim at proving Proposition
5.4
. The strategy of proof is the same followed to prove Proposition
5.3
and many steps will actually be identical. The main differences appear when using Young’s convolution inequality in Fourier space. We, therefore, provide the details only for the steps that differs from the proof of Proposition
5.3
.

Proof.

We split the nonlinear term as in (
5.56
) and we study separately the nonlinear error terms
NL^{e.d.}_{M,B}
(
5.8
) and
NL^{T.d.}_{M,B}
(
5.12
).

5.3.1.
Nonlinear enhanced dissipation errors

In light of the discussion before the inequality (
5.60
), we study the term

(5.129)

\mathcal{I}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M^{\prime}}:=\mathbbm{1}_{|k|\geq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\frac{2^{-\mathtt{C}\beta}}{\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{2\beta}}\left\langle k\right\rangle^{2\alpha}a_{\nu,k}\sum_{N\sim|k|}\left|\left\langle\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M^{\prime}+q_{\gamma,s}}\nabla_{v}Z^{\beta}\mathcal{F}(\Gamma(f_{N},f_{<N/8})),\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M^{\prime}+q_{\gamma,s}}\nabla_{v}Z^{\beta}\hat{f}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\right|.

As in (
5.62
), we split this term as
\mathcal{I}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M^{\prime}}=\mathcal{I}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M^{\prime},\gtrsim\nu}+\mathcal{I}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M^{\prime},\lesssim\nu},
where
\mathcal{I}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M^{\prime},\lesssim\nu}
and
\mathcal{I}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M^{\prime},\gtrsim\nu}
are the right-hand side of (
5.129
) with
f_{<N/8}
replaced by
P_{\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}f_{<N/8}
and
P_{>\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}f_{<N/8}
respectively.

High-moderately low frequency interactions.

We can repeat all the arguments done in (
5.63
)-(
5.70
) by replacing
M
with
M^{\prime}
, since none of these bounds depend on the specific choice of the velocity weight. Hence, recalling the definition (
5.66
)

(5.130)

g^{(\beta,M,j)}=\frac{2^{-\mathtt{C}\beta/2}}{\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{\beta}}\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M+q_{\gamma,s}}(\nabla_{v})^{j}Z^{\beta}\hat{f},

we have

(5.131)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M^{\prime},\gtrsim\nu}\lesssim\frac{1}{\nu}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}N\sim|k|\\
|\beta_{1}|+|\beta_{2}|\leq|\beta|\\
j_{1}+j_{2}\leq 1\end{subarray}}\mathbbm{1}_{|k|\geq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\left\langle k\right\rangle^{\alpha}\sqrt{\nu a_{\nu,k}\mathcal{A}[g^{(\beta,M^{\prime},1)}(k)]}

(5.132)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\bigg(\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{\alpha}\sqrt{\nu(a_{\nu,\xi})^{j_{1}}\mathcal{A}[g^{(\beta_{1},M^{\prime},j_{1})}_{N}(\xi)]}\sqrt{(a_{\nu,k-\xi})^{j_{2}}}\lVert P_{>\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}g^{(\beta_{2},M^{\prime},j_{2})}_{<N/8}(k-\xi)\rVert_{L^{2}_{v}}

(5.133)

\displaystyle+\left\langle\xi\right\rangle^{\alpha}(\sqrt{a_{\nu,\xi}})^{j_{1}}\lVert g^{(\beta_{1},M^{\prime},j_{1})}_{N}(\xi)\rVert_{L^{2}_{v}}\sqrt{\nu(a_{\nu,k-\xi})^{j_{2}}\mathcal{A}[P_{>\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}g^{(\beta_{2},M^{\prime},j_{2})}_{<N/8}(k-\xi)]}\bigg){\rm d}\xi.

We then claim that

(5.134)

\nu\sup_{k\,:\,|k|>\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\sum_{\alpha+|\beta|\leq B^{\prime}}\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle^{2J^{\prime}}\mathcal{I}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M^{\prime},\gtrsim\nu}{\rm d}k\lesssim\sqrt{\mathcal{E}}\sqrt{\mathcal{D}}\sqrt{\mathcal{D}_{LF}}.

To prove this bound, when applying Young’s convolution inequalities to the terms in (
5.132
)-(
5.133
) we can use the
L^{2}_{k}
norm in both factors. This simplifies the proof compared to the one for Proposition
5.3
since we do not need to distinguish cases depending on the number of
Z
-derivatives. Indeed,
having that

(5.135)

J^{\prime}\leq J,\quad M^{\prime}\leq M,\quad\alpha+|\beta_{1}|\leq B^{\prime}\leq B,\quad|\beta_{2}|+1/2\leq B^{\prime}+1\leq B,

since
N\gtrsim\nu
, we get

(5.136)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle^{J^{\prime}}\left\langle k\right\rangle^{\alpha}\sqrt{\nu(a_{\nu,k})^{j_{1}}\mathcal{A}[g^{(\beta_{1},M^{\prime},j_{1})}_{N}]}\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{k}}\lesssim\left\lVert\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle^{J}\sqrt{\mathcal{D}^{e.d.}_{M,B}(k)_{N}}\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{k}}

(5.137)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\sqrt{(a_{\nu,k})^{j_{2}}}P_{>\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}g^{(\beta_{2},M^{\prime},j_{2})}_{<N/8}\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{k}L^{2}_{v}}\lesssim\left\lVert\sqrt{P_{>\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}E^{e.d.}_{M,B}(k)_{<N/8}}\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{k}}

(5.138)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle^{J^{\prime}}\left\langle k\right\rangle^{\alpha}(\sqrt{a_{\nu,k}})^{j_{1}}g^{(\beta_{1},M^{\prime},j_{1})}_{N}\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{k}L^{2}_{v}}\lesssim\left\lVert\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle^{J}\sqrt{E^{e.d.}_{M,B}(k)_{N}}\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{k}}

(5.139)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\sqrt{\nu(a_{\nu,k})^{j_{2}}\mathcal{A}[P_{>\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}g^{(\beta_{2},M^{\prime},j_{2})}_{<N/8}]}\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{k}}\lesssim\left\lVert\sqrt{P_{>\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\mathcal{D}^{e.d.}_{M,B}(k)_{<N/8}}\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{k}}.

Using the Young’s convolution inequality in (
5.131
) and appealing to (
5.136
)-(
5.139
), we have

(5.140)

\displaystyle\nu\sup_{k:\,|k|\geq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\sum_{\alpha+|\beta|\leq B^{\prime}}(\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle^{2J^{\prime}}\mathcal{I}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M^{\prime},\gtrsim\nu})\lesssim\sum_{N\gtrsim\nu}\left\lVert\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle^{J^{\prime}}\sqrt{\mathcal{D}^{e.d.}_{M^{\prime},B^{\prime}}(k)_{\sim N}}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{k}}

(5.141)

\displaystyle\times\bigg(\left\lVert\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle^{J}\sqrt{\mathcal{D}^{e.d.}_{M,B}(k)_{N}}\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{k}}\left\lVert\sqrt{P_{>\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}E^{e.d.}_{M,B}(k)_{<N/8}}\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{k}}

(5.142)

\displaystyle\quad+\left\lVert\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle^{J}\sqrt{E^{e.d.}_{M,B}(k)_{N}}\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{k}}\left\lVert\sqrt{P_{>\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\mathcal{D}^{e.d.}_{M,B}(k)_{<N/8}}\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{k}}\bigg)

(5.143)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\mathcal{E}}\sqrt{\mathcal{D}}\sqrt{\mathcal{D}_{LF}},

where we also used the quasi-orthogonality of the Littlewood-Payley projections. The claim (
5.134
) is proved.

High-very low frequency interactions.

We now turn our attention to
\mathcal{I}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M^{\prime},\lesssim\nu}
. We can repeat the computations in (
5.86
)-(
5.101
) with
M
replaced by
M^{\prime}
. Using the Young’s convolution inequality

(5.144)

\left\lVert g*h\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{k}}\leq\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{k}}\left\lVert h\right\rVert_{L^{1}_{k}},

instead of (
5.103
) we arrive at

(5.145)

\sup_{k:\,|k|\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\sum_{\alpha+|\beta|\leq B^{\prime}}\nu\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle^{2J^{\prime}}\mathcal{I}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M^{\prime},\lesssim\nu}\lesssim\sqrt{\mathcal{E}}\sqrt{\mathcal{D}}\sqrt{\mathcal{D}_{LF}}+\sqrt{\mathcal{E}_{LF}}\sqrt{\mathcal{D}_{LF}}\lVert P_{\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\hat{f}\rVert_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{v}}.

Also in this case we can argue as in (
5.104
) to get

(5.146)

\displaystyle\lVert P_{\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\hat{f}\rVert_{L^{1}_{k}L^{2}_{v}}\lesssim\left(\nu^{d}\mathbbm{1}_{t\leq\nu^{-1}}+\left\langle\nu/t\right\rangle^{\frac{d}{2}}\mathbbm{1}_{t>\nu^{-1}}\right)\sqrt{\mathcal{E}_{LF}}.

Plugging the bound above in (
5.145
) and using (
5.134
) we finally prove the bound (
5.24
) for the term
\mathcal{I}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M^{\prime}}
(
5.129
) in
NL^{e.d.}_{M^{\prime},B^{\prime}}
(
5.8
). The other terms in
NL^{e.d.}_{M^{\prime},B^{\prime}}
can be treated analogously, see the discussion at the end of Section
5.2.1
.

5.3.2.
Nonlinear Taylor dispersion errors

Following the discussion in Section
5.2.2
, we study the term

(5.147)

\mathcal{J}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M^{\prime}}:=\sum_{N\sim N^{\prime}}\mathbbm{1}_{|k|\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\frac{2^{-\mathtt{C}_{1}\beta}}{\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{2\beta}}\left|\left\langle\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M^{\prime}+q_{\gamma,s}}\nabla_{v}Z^{\beta}\mathcal{F}(\Gamma(f_{N},f_{N^{\prime}})),\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M^{\prime}+q_{\gamma,s}}\nabla_{v}Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\right|.

We split it as
\mathcal{J}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M}=\mathcal{J}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M,\lesssim}+\mathcal{J}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M,\gtrsim\nu}
where

\mathcal{J}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M,\lesssim\nu}
and
\mathcal{J}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M,\gtrsim\nu}
are (
5.147
) with
N\sim N^{\prime}\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu
and
N\sim N^{\prime}>\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu
respectively.

Very low-very low frequency interactions
. When
N\lesssim\nu
, we can proceed as done in the previous section (compare with the observation made before (
5.111
)). Thus, we get

(5.148)

\displaystyle\nu\sum_{\alpha+|\beta|\leq B^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\sup_{k:\,|k|\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle^{2J^{\prime}}\mathcal{J}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M^{\prime},\lesssim\nu}

(5.149)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\mathcal{D}_{LF}}\left(\sqrt{\mathcal{E}}\sqrt{\mathcal{D}}+\big(\nu^{d}\mathbbm{1}_{t\leq\nu^{-1}}+\left\langle\nu/t\right\rangle^{\frac{d}{2}}\mathbbm{1}_{t>\nu^{-1}}\big)\mathcal{E}_{LF}\right).

Very low- moderately high frequency interactions
. For
N\gtrsim\nu
, we have the main difference with respect to the
L^{2}_{k}
case. Since we are considering the
L^{\infty}_{k}
-norm, here we cannot gain a smallness parameter as in (
5.113
).
Hence, for the
L^{\infty}_{k}
norm, we have to crucially use the fact that
B^{\prime}<B
. Indeed, since

(5.150)

\nabla_{v}Z^{\beta}=Z^{\beta+1}-t\nabla_{x}Z^{\beta},

by (
5.147
) we can bound
\mathcal{J}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M^{\prime},\gtrsim\nu}
as

\displaystyle\mathbbm{1}_{|k|\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\frac{2^{-\mathtt{C}_{1}\beta}}{\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{2\beta}}\left\langle k\right\rangle^{2\alpha}\sum_{N\sim N^{\prime}\gtrsim\nu}\bigg(\left|\left\langle\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M^{\prime}+q_{\gamma,s}}Z^{\beta+1}\mathcal{F}(\Gamma(f_{N},f_{N^{\prime}})),\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M^{\prime}+q_{\gamma,s}}\nabla_{v}Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\right|

(5.151)

\displaystyle+\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle\left|\left\langle\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M^{\prime}+q_{\gamma,s}}Z^{\beta}\mathcal{F}(\Gamma(f_{N},f_{N^{\prime}})),\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M^{\prime}+q_{\gamma,s}}\nabla_{v}Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\right|\bigg)

where in the last line we used that
t|k|\lesssim\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle
in this regime. Thus, as opposed to (
5.112
), we never have to pay negative powers of
\nu
since we never take a
v
-derivative of the terms
f_{N},f_{N^{\prime}}
(which are in the enhanced dissipation regime). However, we need to use one
Z
-derivative more but we can do so since
B\geq B^{\prime}+1
and we aim at controlling terms with
f_{N},f_{N^{\prime}}
with
L^{2}_{k}
norms.

Remark 5.11
.

This is a term where we are exploiting the phase mixing. Namely, we are controlling a
v
-derivative in terms of a
Z
-derivative.

Notice that in (
5.151
) we have to lose a factor
\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle
also in the term containing
Z^{\beta+1}
and not only in (
5.147
). This is because we have to divide
Z^{\beta+1}
by
\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{\beta+1}
. More precisely, instead of
(
5.112
) we get

(5.152)

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M^{\prime},\gtrsim\nu}\lesssim\frac{1}{\nu}\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}N\sim N^{\prime}\gtrsim\nu\\
|\beta_{1}|+|\beta_{2}|\leq|\beta|+1\end{subarray}}\mathbbm{1}_{|k|\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\left\langle k\right\rangle^{\alpha}\frac{2^{-\mathtt{C}_{1}\beta}}{\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle^{\beta}}\sqrt{\nu\mathcal{A}[\left\langle v\right\rangle^{M^{\prime}+q_{\gamma,s}}\nabla_{v}Z^{\beta}(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}(k)]}

\displaystyle\times\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\bigg(\sqrt{\nu\mathcal{A}[g^{(\beta_{1},M^{\prime},0)}_{N}(\xi)]}\lVert g^{(\beta_{2},M^{\prime},0)}_{N^{\prime}}(k-\xi)\rVert_{L^{2}_{v}}

\displaystyle+\lVert g^{(\beta_{1},M^{\prime},0)}_{N}(\xi)\rVert_{L^{2}_{v}}\sqrt{\nu\mathcal{A}[g^{(\beta_{2},M^{\prime},0)}_{N^{\prime}}(k-\xi)]}\bigg){\rm d}\xi.

We rely on (
5.36
) to absorb the
\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle
loss in (
5.152
). In particular, we have

(5.153)

\displaystyle\nu\sum_{\alpha+|\beta|\leq B^{\prime}}\sup_{k:\,|k|\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle^{2J^{\prime}}\mathcal{J}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M^{\prime},\gtrsim\nu}\lesssim\left\lVert P_{\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle^{J^{\prime}}\sqrt{\mathcal{D}^{T.d.}_{M^{\prime},B^{\prime}}}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{k}}

(5.154)

\displaystyle\times\bigg(\left\lVert P_{>\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle^{J}\sqrt{\mathcal{D}^{e.d.}_{M,B}}\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{k}}\left\lVert P_{>\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\sqrt{E^{e.d.}_{M,B}}\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{k}}

(5.155)

\displaystyle+\left\lVert P_{>\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle^{J}\sqrt{E^{e.d.}_{M,B}}\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{k}}\left\lVert P_{>\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\sqrt{\mathcal{D}^{e.d.}_{M,B}}\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{k}}\bigg)

(5.156)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\mathcal{E}}\sqrt{\mathcal{D}}\sqrt{\mathcal{D}_{LF}}.

Combining together (
5.148
) and (
5.156
), we see that for the term
\mathcal{J}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M^{\prime}}
we get bounds in agreement with (
5.24
). The other terms in (
5.12
)-(
5.16
) that contain at least one
(I-\boldsymbol{P})\hat{f}
are analogous.

The macroscopic error terms defined in (
5.118
) do not depend on the velocity weights. Thus, we can combine the arguments done to obtain (
5.120
) with the ones to get (
5.156
) to prove

(5.157)

\displaystyle\nu\sup_{k:|k|\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\sum_{\alpha+|\beta|\leq B}\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle^{2J}\big(\mathcal{J}^{\alpha,\beta}_{macro,N\sim N^{\prime}}+\frac{c_{0}}{\nu}\mathcal{M}_{\Gamma,N\sim N^{\prime}}\big){\rm d}k

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\mathcal{E}}\sqrt{\mathcal{D}}\sqrt{\mathcal{D}_{LF}},

whence concluding the proof of Proposition
5.4
.
∎

5.4.
Nonlinear errors for the higher order moments

The control for the higher moments in the linearized problem is a straightforward consequence of the estimates (
2.6
) and (
2.15
). For the nonlinear problem, it is also not difficult to adapt the estimates leading to the bounds (
5.23
) and (
5.24
) to obtain (
5.25
) and (
5.26
) respectively. Since we are using higher order velocity weights, we only have to check where we need the functionals
\mathcal{E},\mathcal{E}_{LF}
in (
5.25
) and (
5.26
).

Proof of Proposition
5.6
.

To prove the
L^{2}_{k}
bound (
5.25
), we can argue as done to prove (
5.23
) simply by replacing
(M,J)
with
(M+M_{J},0)
in all the estimates. For instance, in the enhanced dissipation regime we have to bound

(5.158)

\nu\int_{|k|\geq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\sum_{\alpha+|\beta|\leq B}\mathcal{I}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M+M_{J}}{\rm d}k

where
\mathcal{I}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M+M_{J}}
is defined in (
5.60
). With the same splitting introduced in (
5.62
), we first prove (
5.68
) with
M\to M+M_{J}
and then, as in (
5.80
) with
J=0
, we get

(5.159)

\nu\int_{|k|\geq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\sum_{\alpha+|\beta|\leq B}\mathcal{I}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M+M_{J},\gtrsim\nu}{\rm d}k\lesssim\sqrt{\mathcal{E}_{mom}}\mathcal{D}_{mom}.

For the term
\mathcal{I}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M+M_{J},\lesssim\nu}
, we also have (
5.86
) with
M\to M+M_{J}
. For the bound (
5.91
), notice that for the piece with
\boldsymbol{P}f
the weights are irrelevant thanks to the decay of the Maxwellian. These macroscopic terms are the one that needs to be controlled with
\mathcal{E}_{LF}
. We can argue as done to obtain (
5.103
) and (
5.104
) to prove that

(5.160)

\displaystyle\nu\int_{|k|\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha+|\beta|\leq B}\mathcal{I}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M+M_{J},\lesssim\nu}{\rm d}k

(5.161)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\mathcal{E}_{mom}}\sqrt{\mathcal{D}_{mom}}\left(\sqrt{\mathcal{D}_{mom}}+\big(\nu^{d}\mathbbm{1}_{t\leq\nu^{-1}}+\left\langle\nu/t\right\rangle^{\frac{d}{2}}\mathbbm{1}_{t>\nu^{-1}}\big)\sqrt{\mathcal{E}_{LF}}\right).

In the Taylor dispersion regime, consider the term
\mathcal{J}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M+M_{J}}
as in (
5.109
) with its splitting (
5.110
). Following the reasoning before (
5.111
) and the estimates (
5.117
) with
(M,J)\to(M+M_{J},0)
, we get

(5.162)

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha+|\beta|\leq B}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\nu\mathcal{J}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M+M_{J},\lesssim\nu}{\rm d}k

(5.163)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\mathcal{E}_{mom}}\sqrt{\mathcal{D}_{mom}}\left(\sqrt{\mathcal{D}_{mom}}+\big(\nu^{d}\mathbbm{1}_{t\leq\nu^{-1}}+\left\langle\nu/t\right\rangle^{\frac{d}{2}}\mathbbm{1}_{t>\nu^{-1}}\big)\sqrt{\mathcal{E}_{LF}}\right).

Also here, the
\mathcal{E}_{LF}
factor comes from macroscopic error terms, therefore insensitive to the weights.
∎

Proof of Proposition
5.7
.

For the proof of the
L^{\infty}_{k}
bound (
5.26
), we can readily follow the proof of (
5.24
) since the only properties we used are
M^{\prime}\leq M-1,J^{\prime}\leq J-1
and
B^{\prime}\leq B-1
which are still true if we replace
M^{\prime}\to M^{\prime}+M_{J^{\prime}}
and
J^{\prime}\to 0
thanks to (
2.23
). For instance, following (
5.129
)-(
5.146
) and the arguments to get (
5.148
) we obtain

(5.164)

\displaystyle\nu

\displaystyle\sup_{k:\,|k|\geq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\sum_{\alpha+|\beta|\leq B^{\prime}}\mathcal{I}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M^{\prime}+M_{J^{\prime}}}+\mathcal{J}^{\alpha+\beta}_{M^{\prime}+M_{J^{\prime}},\lesssim\nu}

(5.165)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\mathcal{D}_{mom,LF}}\left(\sqrt{\mathcal{E}_{mom}}\sqrt{\mathcal{D}_{mom}}+\big(\nu^{d}\mathbbm{1}_{t\leq\nu^{-1}}+\left\langle\nu/t\right\rangle^{\frac{d}{2}}\mathbbm{1}_{t>\nu^{-1}}\big)\sqrt{\mathcal{E}_{LF}}\sqrt{\mathcal{E}_{mom,LF}}\right).

On the other hand, for
\mathcal{J}^{\alpha+\beta}_{M^{\prime}+M_{J^{\prime}},\gtrsim\nu}
we get the bounds in (
5.152
) with
M^{\prime}\to M^{\prime}+M_{J^{\prime}}
. To absorb the
\left\langle\nu t\right\rangle
we have to use
\mathcal{E}
(or
\mathcal{D}
) instead of
\mathcal{E}_{mom}
since the latter does not contain the factor
\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle
. More precisely, as done to get (
5.153
), we have

(5.166)

\displaystyle\sup_{k:\,|k|\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\sum_{\alpha+|\beta|\leq B^{\prime}}\nu\mathcal{J}^{\alpha,\beta}_{M^{\prime}+M_{J^{\prime}},\gtrsim\nu}\lesssim\left\lVert P_{\leq\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\sqrt{\mathcal{D}^{T.d.}_{M^{\prime}+M_{J^{\prime}},B^{\prime}}}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{k}}

(5.167)

\displaystyle\times\bigg(\left\lVert P_{>\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\sqrt{\mathcal{D}^{e.d.}_{M^{\prime}+M_{J^{\prime}},B^{\prime}}}\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{k}}\left\lVert P_{>\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle\sqrt{E^{e.d.}_{M^{\prime}+M_{J^{\prime}},B^{\prime}}}\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{k}}

(5.168)

\displaystyle+\left\lVert P_{>\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\left\langle\lambda_{\nu,k}t\right\rangle\sqrt{E^{e.d.}_{M^{\prime}+M_{J^{\prime}},B^{\prime}}}\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{k}}\left\lVert P_{>\delta_{0}^{-1}\nu}\sqrt{\mathcal{D}^{e.d.}_{M^{\prime}+M_{J^{\prime}},B^{\prime}}}\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{k}}\bigg)

(5.169)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\mathcal{E}}\sqrt{\mathcal{D}_{mom}}\sqrt{\mathcal{D}_{mom,LF}},

where in the last inequality we used
M^{\prime}+M_{J^{\prime}}\leq M
and
J\geq 1
.
Finally, all the error terms that do not have velocity weights are clearly bounded in the same way, for instance the ones in (
5.118
). Hence, the proof of Proposition
5.7
is over.
∎

Appendix A
Basic inequalities

To handle to anisotropic dissipation in the problem under consideration in this paper, we need the following lower bound.

Lemma A.1
.

Let
R>1
,
0<s\leq 1
and
\gamma<0
. Then

(A.1)

\left\lVert\mathbbm{1}_{|v|\leq R}g\right\rVert^{2}_{H^{s}_{\gamma/2}}\gtrsim\frac{1}{R^{|\gamma|(2-s)}}\left\lVert\mathbbm{1}_{|v|\leq R}g\right\rVert^{2}_{H^{s}}.

Proof.

Consider first
s<1
. Let
0<\delta<1
be a small parameter to be chosen. Then

(A.2)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbbm{1}_{|v|\leq R}g\right\rVert^{2}_{H^{s}_{\gamma/2}}

\displaystyle\geq\left\lVert\mathbbm{1}_{|v|\leq R}g\right\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2}}+\delta\left\lVert\mathbbm{1}_{|v|\leq R}g\right\rVert^{2}_{\dot{H}^{s}_{\gamma/2}}

Since
(a+b)^{2}\geq a^{2}/2-b^{2}
, we get

(A.3)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbbm{1}_{|v|\leq R}g\right\rVert^{2}_{\dot{H}^{s}_{\gamma/2}}

\displaystyle=\iint\frac{\left(\left\langle v\right\rangle^{\frac{\gamma}{2}}(\mathbbm{1}_{|v|\leq R}g-\mathbbm{1}_{|v^{\prime}|\leq R}g^{\prime})+\mathbbm{1}_{|v^{\prime}|\leq R}g^{\prime}(\left\langle v\right\rangle^{\frac{\gamma}{2}}-\left\langle v^{\prime}\right\rangle^{\frac{\gamma}{2}})\right)^{2}}{|v-v^{\prime}|^{d+2s}}{\rm d}v{\rm d}v^{\prime}

(A.4)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\iint\left\langle v\right\rangle^{\gamma}\frac{(\mathbbm{1}_{|v|\leq R}g-\mathbbm{1}_{|v^{\prime}|\leq R}g^{\prime})^{2}}{|v-v^{\prime}|^{d+2s}}{\rm d}v{\rm d}v^{\prime}

(A.5)

\displaystyle\quad-\iint\left(\mathbbm{1}_{|v-v^{\prime}|<R^{q}}+\mathbbm{1}_{|v-v^{\prime}|\geq R^{q}}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{|v^{\prime}|\leq R}(g^{\prime})^{2}\frac{(\left\langle v\right\rangle^{\frac{\gamma}{2}}-\left\langle v^{\prime}\right\rangle^{\frac{\gamma}{2}})^{2}}{|v-v^{\prime}|^{d+2s}}{\rm d}v{\rm d}v^{\prime}

(A.6)

\displaystyle:=I^{1}-I_{<R^{q}}^{2}-I_{\geq R^{q}}^{2},

where
q>0
is a constant to be chosen later. For
I_{1}
, recalling that
\gamma<0
, one has

(A.7)

I_{1}=\frac{1}{4}\iint(\left\langle v\right\rangle^{\gamma}+\langle{v^{\prime}}\rangle^{\gamma})\frac{(\mathbbm{1}_{|v|\leq R}g-\mathbbm{1}_{|v^{\prime}|\leq R}g^{\prime})^{2}}{|v-v^{\prime}|^{d+2s}}{\rm d}v{\rm d}v^{\prime}\gtrsim\frac{1}{R^{|\gamma|}}\left\lVert\mathbbm{1}_{|v|\leq R}g\right\rVert^{2}_{\dot{H}^{s}},

where in the last bound we used that on the support of the integral we cannot have
|v|\geq R
and
|v^{\prime}|\geq R
.
To control
I^{2}_{<R^{q}}
, by the mean value theorem we get

(A.8)

\mathbbm{1}_{|v-v^{\prime}|<1}(\left\langle v\right\rangle^{\frac{\gamma}{2}}-\left\langle v^{\prime}\right\rangle^{\frac{\gamma}{2}})^{2}\lesssim\mathbbm{1}_{|v-v^{\prime}|<1}|v-v^{\prime}|^{2}(\left\langle v\right\rangle^{\gamma-2}+\left\langle v^{\prime}\right\rangle^{\gamma-2})\lesssim\mathbbm{1}_{|v-v^{\prime}|<1}|v-v^{\prime}|^{2}\left\langle v^{\prime}\right\rangle^{\gamma}.

Therefore, since
s<1
we have

(A.9)

|I_{<R^{q}}^{2}|\lesssim\int\mathbbm{1}_{|v^{\prime}|\leq R}\left\langle v^{\prime}\right\rangle^{\gamma}(g^{\prime})^{2}{\rm d}v^{\prime}\int\mathbbm{1}_{|v-v^{\prime}|<R^{q}}\frac{1}{|v-v^{\prime}|^{3-2(1-s)}}{\rm d}v\lesssim R^{2q(1-s)}\left\lVert\mathbbm{1}_{|v|\leq R}g\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2}}^{2}.

For the remaining term

(A.10)

\displaystyle|I_{\geq R^{q}}^{2}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int\mathbbm{1}_{|v^{\prime}|\leq R}(g^{\prime})^{2}{\rm d}v^{\prime}\int\mathbbm{1}_{|v-v^{\prime}|>R^{q}}\frac{1}{|v-v^{\prime}|^{d+2s}}{\rm d}v\lesssim R^{-2qs}\left\lVert\mathbbm{1}_{|v|\leq R}g\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{v}}^{2}.

Thus, combining (
A.7
), (
A.9
) and (
A.10
) we deduce that there is a constant
C
such that

(A.11)

\left\lVert\mathbbm{1}_{|v|\leq R}g\right\rVert^{2}_{\dot{H}_{\gamma/2}}\geq\frac{1}{CR^{|\gamma|}}\left\lVert\mathbbm{1}_{|v|\leq R}g\right\rVert^{2}_{\dot{H}^{s}}-CR^{2q(1-s)}\left\lVert\mathbbm{1}_{|v|\leq R}g\right\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2}}-CR^{-2qs}\left\lVert\mathbbm{1}_{|v|\leq R}g\right\rVert_{L^{2}_{v}}^{2}.

Choosing

(A.12)

\delta=cR^{-2q(1-s)},\qquad c=1/(4C),\qquad q=|\gamma|/2,

and plugging (
A.11
) in (
A.2
), we get

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbbm{1}_{|v|\leq R}g\right\rVert^{2}_{H^{s}_{\gamma/2}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{3}{4}\left\lVert\mathbbm{1}_{|v|\leq R}g\right\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2}}+\frac{1}{4C^{2}R^{|\gamma|(2-s)}}\left\lVert\mathbbm{1}_{|v|\leq R}g\right\rVert^{2}_{\dot{H}^{s}}-\frac{1}{4R^{|\gamma|}}\left\lVert\mathbbm{1}_{|v|\leq R}g\right\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2R^{|\gamma|}}\left\lVert\mathbbm{1}_{|v|\leq R}g\right\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}}+\frac{1}{4C^{2}R^{|\gamma|(2-s)}}\left\lVert\mathbbm{1}_{|v|\leq R}g\right\rVert^{2}_{\dot{H}^{s}}.

Since
2-s>1
, the proof for
s<1
is over. When
s=1
, we can explicitly compute the commutator
[\nabla_{v},\left\langle v\right\rangle^{\gamma/2}]
and we omit the details of this simpler proof.
∎

For convenience of the reader, we recall here some basic inequalities used in
[
4
,
5
]
and that we exploit in Section
3
.

Lemma A.2

(
[
5
]
*Lemma 2.5 )
.

For any
\alpha>-3
,
\rho>0,\,\alpha\in\mathbb{R}
one has

(A.13)

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|w|^{\alpha}\left\langle w\right\rangle^{\beta}\left\langle w+u\right\rangle^{\alpha}\mu^{\rho}(w+u){\rm d}w\approx\left\langle u\right\rangle^{\alpha+\beta}.

Lemma A.3

(
[
4
]
*Lemma 2.5)
.

For any
g\in C^{1}_{b}
one has

(A.14)

\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos(\theta))|g(v_{*})-g(v_{*}^{\prime})|{\rm d}\sigma\leq C_{g}\left\langle v-v_{*}\right\rangle^{2s}.

When
0<s\leq 1/2
one can replace
\left\langle v-v_{*}\right\rangle
with
|v-v_{*}|
.

Proof.

By (
1.3
), observe that

(A.15)

|v_{*}^{\prime}-v_{*}|^{2}=\frac{|v-v_{*}|^{2}}{2}\left(1-\frac{(v-v_{*})\cdot\sigma}{|v-v_{*}|}\right)=\frac{|v-v_{*}|^{2}}{2}\left(1-\cos(\theta)\right)=\sin(\theta/2)^{2}|v-v_{*}|^{2}.

Using Taylor’s formula

(A.16)

|g(v_{*})-g(v_{*}^{\prime})|\leq C_{g}|v_{*}-v_{*}^{\prime}|=C_{g}\sin(\theta/2)|v-v_{*}|.

Let
0<\delta<\pi/2
. Combining the inequality above with the property (
1.8
), we infer

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos(\theta))|g(v_{*})-g(v_{*}^{\prime}))|{\rm d}\sigma

\displaystyle\lesssim_{g}|v-v_{*}|\int_{0}^{\delta}\frac{\sin(\theta/2)}{\theta^{2s+1}}{\rm d}\theta+\int_{\delta}^{\pi/2}\frac{1}{\theta^{1+2s}}{\rm d}\theta

\displaystyle\lesssim_{g}|v-v_{*}|\delta^{-2s+1}+\delta^{-2s}

If
|v-v_{*}|<1
then choose
\delta=1/10
and use
|v-v_{*}|\leq 1\leq\left\langle v-v_{*}\right\rangle^{2s}
. If
s\leq 1/2
then it is also true
|v-v_{*}|\leq|v-v_{*}|^{2s}
. When
|v-v_{*}|>1
, take
\delta=|v-v_{*}|^{-1}
.
∎
